Expected: FAIL with `Error: Cannot find module '../../src/primitives/advance.js' imported from '.../test/primitives/advance.test.ts'` (the headline; vitest's cause line beneath it is `Failed to load url ../../src/primitives/advance.js`, as P8-T3 and P8-T4 recorded for a direct import of the test file; the test imports the planner above its helper and oracle imports, so this is the first unresolved module).

- [ ] **Step 2: The block-mapped body, and the uniformity trap it walks into**

Each workgroup loads up to `WG` frontier vertices, reads their degrees (clipped to the bound arc window, so P8-T12 changes nothing in the body), runs the prelude's inclusive workgroup scan `wg_scan_u32` over those degrees (P8-T1 Step 5: the subgroup form when the device has the feature, the Hillis-Steele form otherwise -- which is the ONLY thing that differs between the twin's two compilations), and then every invocation strips the range `[0, aggregate)` by binary search (`upper_bound`) over the scanned degrees to find which source vertex its arc belongs to. The trap: the guarded loads (`if (i < count)`) must write into locals and the scan call must sit UNCONDITIONALLY after the guard, because the helper's `workgroupBarrier` inside the guard is rejected by Tint with "must only be called from uniform control flow". This is rule 1 of the package's WGSL conventions and it is the single most likely reason this body will not compile on the first try. The aggregate is read with `workgroupUniformLoad`, which makes it a uniform value (and carries its own barrier), so a strip loop bounded by it is uniform control flow -- this body needs no barrier inside the loop, but `bfs-fused` (P8-T7), which copies the loop textually, does.

```ts
export const advanceExpandWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
var<workgroup> sh: array<u32, WG>;          // the block's degrees, then their inclusive scan
var<workgroup> rowStart: array<u32, WG>;    // the first bound arc of each entry's row
var<workgroup> base: u32;                   // the block's reserved span in the edge queue

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn advance_expand(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let g = group_id(wid);
    let i = g * WG + lid.x;                                          // this lane's frontier entry
    let count = atomicLoad(&counters[0]);                            // frontierCount
    var deg = 0u;
    var start = 0u;
    if (i < count) {                                                 // guarded loads into locals (3.5 rule 1)
        let v = frontierIn[i];
        let lo = max(rowPtr[v], P.arcBase);                          // the row clipped to the bound window (P8-T12)
        let hi = min(rowPtr[v + 1u], P.arcEnd);
        start = lo;
        deg = select(0u, hi - lo, hi > lo);
    }
    let inclusive = wg_scan_u32(deg, lid.x);                         // the prelude's inclusive scan in LANE order (P8-T1 Step 5); the twin
    sh[lid.x] = inclusive;                                           // sh is monotone in lid.x, the index the binary search walks
    rowStart[lid.x] = start;
    workgroupBarrier();
    let aggregate = workgroupUniformLoad(&sh[WG - 1u]);              // uniform; includes a barrier
    if (lid.x == 0u) {
        base = atomicAdd(&counters[8], aggregate);                   // edgeCount: ONE reservation per workgroup, not one per arc
        atomicAdd(&counters[9], aggregate);                          // edgeCountUnclamped: the overflow detector (PD-23)
        atomicAdd(&counters[2], aggregate);                          // frontierDegreeSum: Beamer's m_f (P8-T8)
    }
    workgroupBarrier();
    for (var p = lid.x; p < aggregate; p = p + WG) {                 // strip [0, aggregate): lane j takes j, j + WG, ...
        var lo = 0u;                                                 // upper_bound: the first k with sh[k] > p owns arc p
        var hi = WG;
        loop {
            if (lo >= hi) { break; }
            let mid = (lo + hi) / 2u;
            if (sh[mid] > p) { hi = mid; } else { lo = mid + 1u; }
        }
        let k = lo;
        var exclusive = 0u;
        if (k > 0u) { exclusive = sh[k - 1u]; }
        let arc = rowStart[k] + (p - exclusive);
        let q = base + p;
        if (q < P.edgeCapacity) { edgeQueue[q] = colIdx[arc - P.arcBase]; }   // the clamp of PD-23; the queue holds the target vertex only (PD-24)
    }
}
`;
```

The queue holds one word per arc, the target vertex: the contract needs nothing else once `parent` comes from the post-pass (PD-24), and that halves the queue against a `(u, v)` pair and is what keeps 20M arcs at 80 MB.

Registry entry: `id: "advance-expand"`, `entryPoint: "advance_expand"`, bindings: the four graph slots (group 0), `(1,0) frontierIn storage-ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters storage array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) edgeQueue storage array<u32>`, `(2,0) P uniform FrontierParams`; `overrideDecls: []`; `needs: ["subgroups"]`; `phase: "P8"`. Storage count 7.

- [ ] **Step 3: The workgroup-per-row tier and the subgroup twin**

Design 6 row 8's "WORKGROUP_PER_ROW tier for rows above 1,024 arcs" is the structure of `bfs-fused` (P8-T7): one workgroup per frontier entry, every lane striding that entry's row with the claim inline. It is the small-frontier kernel, and a hub-only frontier -- the 10,000-degree star's level 0 -- is a small frontier: the gate's "a single row through the workgroup tier" is that dispatch. The block-mapped body above is the large-frontier kernel, and inside a block it already balances a hub row over all `WG` lanes (a 10k-degree entry costs its block 40 strips, never one lane 10,000 reads), so a second row-per-workgroup variant of `advance-expand` would duplicate `bfs-fused`'s loop for no consumer; `advance-expand` therefore declares NO `TIER` override, and the tier choice IS the fused / two-phase choice of P8-T7. Say this in the JSDoc so the design's three-way row is traceable.

The subgroup twin is NOT a second body: it is the same body compiled against the subgroup helper block, so `wg_scan_u32` resolves to `subgroupExclusiveAdd` / `subgroupAdd` plus the elected-lane slot carry (P8-T1 Step 5) instead of Hillis-Steele. The composer splices that block when `needs` lists `"subgroups"` and the device has the feature, and the workgroup-memory block otherwise (`GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS=1` forces the latter); a body that inlined its own scan would compile to identical text on and off and the twin test of P8-T15 Step 3 would compare a kernel with itself. Because the helper returns each lane's prefix in LANE order whichever form it takes, `sh` is monotone in `lid.x` without re-indexing and the arcs land in the queue in the same positions under both forms; the suite still compares SORTED queues, because the queue's order across WORKGROUPS is the `atomicAdd` reservation order and that is schedule-dependent under either form. The registry entry's `needs: ["subgroups"]` is therefore load-bearing, and `test/kernel/wgsl.test.ts`'s "helper call without needs" case is what refuses a body that drops it.

- [ ] **Step 4: The planner**

`prepareAdvance(scope, core)` calls `assertNotWindowed(core, "advance")` (until P8-T12) and compiles `kernelSpec("advance-expand", graphOverrides(core, null))` once. `record(pass, frontier, level, { arcBase, arcEnd })` validates the level, the window and the frontier's `n` against the core, writes one `FRONTIER_PARAMS` record (`role 0` unused, `edgeCapacity = frontier.edgeCapacity`, `n`, `wg`, the window), binds `frontierIn = frontier.input`, `counters`, `edgeQueue` and the graph bindings from `graphBindings(core, null)` -- `Kernel.bind` already reuses the bind groups of the same buffers, so the two sides of a frontier cost two cached bind-group sets and the planner keeps no cache of its own -- and calls `kernel.dispatchIndirect(pass, bound, frontier.args, level * FRONTIER_CANDIDATES + SLOT.expand, [params.offset])`. Until P8-T12 the CALLER passes `arcBase 0, arcEnd arcCount` (the test helper does).

- [ ] **Step 5: The oracle and the differential suite**

`test/oracle/advance.ts` is a nested loop: for each vertex in the frontier, for each arc, emit the target. The kernel's edge queue is a MULTISET in an order the schedule chooses, so the test sorts both sides before comparing -- and asserts the run-twice check on the SORTED queue plus on `edgeCount`, `edgeCountUnclamped` and `frontierDegreeSum` raw, which are order-independent sums. Every case reads the `edgeQueue` buffer (`readU32` over `frontier.edgeQueue`, the first `edgeCount` words) and the counters block by its own binding, and drives `frontier-finalize` role 0 (`mode 1, fusedMax 0`) to write the expand slot before the indirect dispatch. Fixtures: the empty frontier (records nothing and leaves the three counters 0), a single-vertex frontier, the whole vertex set of `KARATE_EDGES`, a `starEdges(10000)` hub as the only frontier entry, `gridEdges(100, 100)` at every level of a real BFS (the oracle's levels), and `rmatEdges(16, 10, 1)` with the whole vertex set. Each with the subgroup twin in-process (a second context from `acquire({ subgroups: false })`), asserting the two agree bitwise on the sorted queue.

Then the gate's overflow case (PD-23): construct a `Frontier` with `edgeCapacity` FAKED to 4,096 and expand the star's hub. Assert `edgeCountUnclamped === 10000` (the counters block), `edgeCount === 10000` before role 1 and `4096` after it, the first 4,096 queue words are a sub-multiset of the oracle's (sorted comparison), and role 1 wrote `(0, 0, 1, 0)` into `SLOT.contract`, real args (`(1, 1, 1, 1)`: one workgroup for the one entry) into `SLOT.fusedRetry` and `overflowLevels === 1`.

Built 2026-09-24: `test/helpers/advance.ts` drives every case through `runAdvance(ctx, s, frontiers, { edgeCapacity?, role1? })` -- one scope and two planners per call, one submit per frontier, the edge queue and the args poisoned before each, `reset(queue, 0, { nextFrontierCount: count, level: U32_MAX })` then the list written into `frontier.input` -- and reads back the counters block, the first `min(edgeCount, capacity)` queue words plus the word after them (asserted still poison: the kernel writes exactly the reserved span) and the args. `rmatEdges(16, 10, 1)` is built with `snapshotOf(edges, { nodeCount: 65536 })`: the generator leaves 55 indices unused, so the default node count is 65,481 and the whole set would not be 2^16; the pinned count also puts zero-degree entries into the frontier. The empty frontier is asserted through `done === 1`, the three counters 0, a queue of length 0 and the poison at word 0. The twin runs the six fixtures, not the overflow case. `advanceReport` (the sabotage check) is the REVERSED karate vertex set, five levels of the 20 x 20 grid, the star hub and the overflow case: on an index-ordered frontier `rowStart[k - 1] + deg[k - 1]` IS `rowStart[k]`, so a lower-bound search reads the right arc at every boundary and the `lower-bound-not-upper` row would SURVIVE karate in index order and the rmat set; the reversed order and the grid levels are what catch it.

- [ ] **Step 6: The sabotage rows (five, design 13 rule (f))**

```ts
    "advance-expand": Object.freeze([
        { name: "scan-result-dropped", find: "sh[lid.x] = inclusive;", replace: "sh[lid.x] = deg;", minFactor: 10, test: ADVANCE_TEST },
        { name: "lower-bound-not-upper", find: "if (sh[mid] > p) { hi = mid; } else { lo = mid + 1u; }", replace: "if (sh[mid] >= p) { hi = mid; } else { lo = mid + 1u; }", minFactor: 10, test: ADVANCE_TEST },
        { name: "per-lane-reservation", find: "if (lid.x == 0u) {\n        base = atomicAdd(&counters[8], aggregate);", replace: "{\n        base = atomicAdd(&counters[8], aggregate);", minFactor: 10, test: ADVANCE_TEST },
        { name: "last-entry-skipped", find: "if (i < count) {                                                 // guarded loads into locals (3.5 rule 1)", replace: "if (i + 1u < count) {", minFactor: 10, test: ADVANCE_TEST },
```

The first stores the unscanned degrees, so the binary search walks a non-monotone array and every lane gets the wrong source (the helper itself is prelude code and is not a per-kernel row); the second is off by one at every block boundary (caught by the reversed frontier and the grid levels, NOT by an index-ordered frontier, Step 5); the third reserves the aggregate once per lane so the queue interleaves and `edgeCount` explodes; the fourth drops the LAST entry of the frontier (`i + 1 < count` fails only for the lane holding it), whose arcs are then missing; the fifth is caught by the overflow case AND by every other case, because each pins `edgeCountUnclamped` equal to the oracle's total beside `edgeCount`. Each must fail `test/primitives/advance.test.ts` (a sorted-queue or counter miss reports `Infinity`), and `test/sabotage/coverage.test.ts` asserts each `find` string occurs exactly once in the live body. Create `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts` with the fixture loop of Step 5 against `bfsOracle`.

Run: `cd $PKG && eval $GPU_LLVM pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts`
Expected: FAIL with `Error: Cannot find module '../../src/algorithms/bfs.js' imported from '.../test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts'` (the headline; vitest's cause line beneath it is `Failed to load url ../../src/algorithms/bfs.js`, as P8-T3, P8-T4 and P8-T5 recorded for a direct import of the test file; the driver import sorts above the `../helpers/bfs.js` import, so it is the first unresolved module). Built 2026-09-24: the case list below is written with `normaliseMaxDepth` absent from the driver too, and the normalisation case then goes red with `UniformBlock: field "maxDepth" is not a u32` on the first illegal raw value the case order reaches (`-1` there; `Infinity` fails with the same message).

- [ ] **Step 2: PD-6 -- the claim, and why there is no retry loop**

`depth` is `array<atomic<u32>>` initialised to `INVALID_INDEX` by a `fill` dispatch (mode 0, value `INVALID_INDEX`), then `depth[source] = 0` by `queue.writeBuffer` after that batch's submit. The contract phase, over the edge queue, does:

```ts
export const bfsContractWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
var<workgroup> sh: array<u32, WG>;
var<workgroup> base: u32;

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bfs_contract(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let i = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    let count = atomicLoad(&counters[8]);                            // edgeCount, clamped by role 1
    let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]) + 1u;                      // the depth this level assigns
    var won = 0u;
    var v = 0u;
    if (i < count) {                                                 // guarded work into locals
        v = edgeQueue[i];
        let old = atomicMin(&depth[v], claim);
        won = select(0u, 1u, old == INVALID_INDEX);                  // PD-6: the unique winner
    }
    sh[lid.x] = won;
    workgroupBarrier();
    for (var s = 1u; s < WG; s = s * 2u) {                           // Hillis-Steele inclusive scan of won
        var t = 0u;
        if (lid.x >= s) { t = sh[lid.x - s]; }
        workgroupBarrier();
        sh[lid.x] = sh[lid.x] + t;
        workgroupBarrier();
    }
    let inclusive = sh[lid.x];
    if (lid.x == WG - 1u) { base = atomicAdd(&counters[1], inclusive); }   // nextFrontierCount: one reservation per workgroup
    workgroupBarrier();
    if (won == 1u) { frontierOut[base + inclusive - 1u] = v; }
}
`;
```

The invocation that observes `INVALID_INDEX` is the unique winner, because `atomicMin` is a single atomic read-modify-write and only the first one at this level can see the sentinel; a second lane at the same level observes `claim`, a vertex already at a smaller depth returns that depth and keeps it. Design 8.4 is explicit about why this is not `atomicCompareExchangeWeak`: WGSL 17.8.5 says it "may spuriously fail on some implementations", so a compare-exchange claim needs a retry loop, and a vertex could go unclaimed for its level if the loop is bounded. `atomicMin` has no such failure mode. Nothing here writes a parent (PD-24). Registry: `(1,0) edgeQueue ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) depth rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,3) frontierOut rw array<u32>`, `(2,0) P FrontierParams`; storage count 4; no graph group.

- [ ] **Step 3: PD-24 -- `parent` from one post-pass, and PD-5, why no dedupe here**

`parent[v]` is the SMALLEST `u` with `depth[u] + 1 == depth[v]` and an arc `u -> v`, written after the traversal by `sssp-pred` in its depth mode, one thread per row, `atomicMin` on `pred[v]` (filled with `INVALID_INDEX` first). That makes `parent` bitwise reproducible (the CPU's FIFO parent is a valid predecessor but not this one, so the tests compare by the level rule and `expectSmallestPredecessor`, never by equality), removes a binding from every claim kernel, and gives `sssp` its `predArc` from the same body in `MODE 0` -- where `MODE 0` follows PD-27's key (P8-T9 Step 5 states the rule and its proof; the body below carries every role of it, so the registry entry is written once). The depth-mode chain is acyclic on its own: `du + 1 == dv` steps to a strictly smaller depth, so `parent` needs none of the hop machinery, and P8-T9's unit-weight route (the same mode with `predKind 0`) inherits that.

```ts
export const ssspPredWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn sssp_pred(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let first = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    let hb = ((P.n + 63u) / 64u) * 64u;                              // MODE 0 only: hops[v] is pred[hb + v]; pred[2 hb] is the changed word, pred[2 hb + 1] the orphan word (PD-27)
    if (P.role == 1u && P.iteration != 0u && atomicLoad(&pred[2u * hb]) < P.iteration) { return; }   // the previous hop pass changed nothing: converged (no barrier anywhere, so the early return is legal)
    for (var u = first; u < P.n; u = u + P.stride) {                 // grid-stride over the rows (planGridStride(n)); no barrier anywhere
        let du = dist[u];
        var unreached = false;
        if (MODE == 1u) { unreached = du == INVALID_INDEX; } else { unreached = du == F32_INF_BITS; }
        if (unreached) { continue; }
        var hu = 0u;                                                 // u's hop count (MODE 0, roles 1 and 2)
        if (MODE == 0u && P.role != 0u) {
            hu = atomicLoad(&pred[hb + u]);
            if (hu == INVALID_INDEX) {                               // the key has not reached u yet
                if (P.role == 2u && u != P.source) { atomicAdd(&pred[2u * hb + 1u], 1u); }   // an orphan: only bellmanFord can make one (P8-T10 Step 3)
                continue;
            }
        }
        let end = min(rowPtr[u + 1u], P.arcEnd);
        for (var a = max(rowPtr[u], P.arcBase); a < end; a = a + 1u) {
            let v = colIdx[a - P.arcBase];
            if (v == P.source) { continue; }                         // the source keeps INVALID_INDEX whatever attains it (a zero-weight arc could)
            let dv = dist[v];
            var tight = false;                                       // the arc explains dist[v]
            var below = false;                                       // and its source sits at a strictly smaller distance
            if (MODE == 1u) {
                tight = (du + 1u) == dv;                             // depth mode: BFS parent, one depth down
            } else {
                let w = select(1.0, weights[a - P.arcBase], HAS_WEIGHTS);
                tight = (dv != F32_INF_BITS) && (bitcast<u32>(bitcast<f32>(du) + w) == dv);   // one f32 add, compared as the bit pattern PD-9 stores; never into an unreached v (an overflowed sum is +Inf too)
                below = bitcast<f32>(du) < bitcast<f32>(dv);
            }
            if (!tight) { continue; }
            var admit = false;                                       // this arc is one key step below v
            if (MODE == 1u) {
                admit = true;
            } else if (P.role == 0u) {                               // the roots pass (the plateau rule only; bellmanFord seeds the source alone)
                if (P.mode == 0u && below) { atomicMin(&pred[hb + v], 0u); }
            } else if (P.role == 1u) {                               // a hop pass: one hop along a plateau arc (mode 0) or along any tight arc (mode 1)
                if (P.mode == 1u || du == dv) {
                    let old = atomicMin(&pred[hb + v], hu + 1u);
                    if (hu + 1u < old) { atomicMax(&pred[2u * hb], P.iteration + 1u); }   // this pass changed something
                }
            } else {                                                 // the predecessor pass: the smallest tight arc one key step below v
                let hv = atomicLoad(&pred[hb + v]);
                if (P.mode == 1u) { admit = hu + 1u == hv; } else if (hv == 0u) { admit = below; } else { admit = (du == dv) && (hu + 1u == hv); }
            }
            if (admit) { atomicMin(&pred[v], select(a, u, P.predKind == 1u)); }
        }
    }
}
`;
```

The body STRIDES, because the driver dispatches it over `planGridStride(n)` and that plan caps the dispatch at 64 workgroups on a software adapter and 4,096 on hardware (`src/kernel/dispatch.ts`: 16,384 and 1,048,576 rows at `WG = 256`); a per-invocation body under that plan would leave every row above the cap at `INVALID_INDEX`, silently, and no fixture below the `node-limits` project would notice. `P.stride` is the plan's `stride` (`FrontierParams` @64), written by the driver exactly as `bfs-unvisited-flags` (P8-T8 Step 2) and `bf-relax` (P8-T10 Step 2) receive theirs; P8-T12's per-window dispatch of the same body keeps the plan and the stride and changes only `arcBase` / `arcEnd`. `FrontierParams` carries `source` at @60, `role` at @0, `mode` at @36 and `iteration` at @72 (P8-T4's block already declares them). Registry: the four graph slots, `(1,0) dist ro array<u32>` (the traversal's atomic array, bound plain across dispatches), `(1,1) pred rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(2,0) P`; `overrideDecls: [{ name: "MODE", type: "u32", default: 0 }]`; storage count 6; compile pin 9. In `MODE 1` the body reads none of `P.role`, `P.mode` and `P.iteration` and never touches `pred` beyond word `n - 1`, so the BFS driver's `n`-word `pred` and zeroed fields are right. In `MODE 0` `pred` is the `2 x hb + 64`-word buffer of P8-T9 Step 5 (`hb = roundUp(n, 64)`): `pred[0, n)` the arcs, `pred[hb, hb + n)` the hop counts, `pred[2 hb]` the changed word and `pred[2 hb + 1]` the orphan word -- the two-region idiom `queueOut` (P8-T9) and `sweepIn` (P8-T8) already use, so the chain machinery costs no binding and no field: the layout is computed in the body from `P.n`.

The edge queue contains duplicates: a vertex with three frontier neighbours appears three times. All three run the claim; exactly one wins; exactly one appends. The output vertex frontier is duplicate-free by construction, so design 8.4's ownership dedupe has nothing to remove (DEP-P8-B). Say this in the driver's JSDoc, because the next reader will otherwise wonder where the dedupe went.

- [ ] **Step 4: The host loop (PD-7) and the deterministic `order` (PD-14)**

```
ctx.assertReady(); await assertDeviceComputes(ctx);
validate source (an integer in [0, n), else E_INVALID_ARGUMENT { argument: "source" }), dest (a U32 of length n, or absent), signal
maxDepthU32 = normaliseMaxDepth(options.maxDepth)                     // the host rule below; the ONLY value that reaches the uniform
core = ctx.residency.core(s); assertWholeCore(core, ...) until P8-T12
scope = algorithmScope(ctx, "breadthFirstSearch", RING_SLOTS)       // RING_SLOTS: counted below, pinned in bfs.ts
planner = await prepareFrontier(scope, n, arcCount, tuning.edgeCapacity); advance = await prepareAdvance(scope, core); contract, pred, fill = pipelines
depth = scope.scratch(4n); setup batch: fill depth with INVALID_INDEX; submit; await; queue.writeBuffer(depth, 4 * source, [0])
frontier.reset(queue, source, { nextFrontierCount: 1, level: U32_MAX })   // P8-T4 Step 4: the first boundary rotates the source in
for each submit (while not done):
    batch = new CommandBatch(ctx, "breadthFirstSearch/levels"); pass = batch.pass("bfs")
    for level in 0 .. levelsPerSubmit - 1:
        planner.recordFinalize(pass, 0, level, { ...fields, firstOfSubmit: Math.min(level, 2) })   // the level boundary: rotates the counts, chooses, writes this level's slots; the clamped boundary index gates the unvisited subtractions (P8-T8 Step 2)
        advance.record(pass, frontier, level, window)                // indirect, SLOT.expand
        planner.recordFinalize(pass, 1, level, fields)              // the edge-queue role: clamps edgeCount, writes SLOT.contract or SLOT.fusedRetry
        contract.dispatchIndirect(pass, boundFor(frontier.side), frontier.args, level * 7 + SLOT.contract, [params])
        frontier.swap()
    batch.endPass()
    request = batch.readback(frontier.counters.buffer, 4 * W.done, 4)   // the four bytes
    submitted = batch.submit(); bytes = await submitted.readback; ctx.assertReady(); signal check (E_ABORTED with batchId)
    onProgress(levelsRecordedSoFar, n)
    if (submits > n + 1) throw E_VALIDATION (a traversal has at most n levels; an unset done flag is a kernel bug, never a hang)
result batch: keys = scope.scratch(4n); batch.copy(depth -> keys); vals = fill mode 1 (iota); radixSort.record(pass, keys, vals, n, 32, scratch) -> sorted;
              pred = fill INVALID_INDEX; sssp-pred MODE 1, predKind tuning.predKind ?? 1 over plan = planGridStride(n, WG, caps) with stride = plan.stride and source (the pass ignores role, mode and iteration in MODE 1, Step 3); four readback requests: depth (4n), pred (4n), sorted.vals (4n), counters (96)
              submit; ONE mapAsync (the batch stages every request into one slot, CommandBatch.readback / submit)
levels = counters.frontierCount === 0 ? counters.level : counters.level + 1      // done by an empty frontier, or by maxDepth with a reached-but-unexpanded level
order = new Uint32Array(resultBytes, valsOffset, counters.visitedCount)           // grouped by depth (the sort key), ascending by index within a depth (the iota values, stable sort), INVALID_INDEX keys last
```

An empty frontier makes every following level's args `(0, 0, 1)`, so the extra recorded levels are no-ops and the loop is correct without knowing the diameter -- that is the property design 5.4 exists for. `maxDepth` is a `FrontierParams` `u32` field that role 0 turns into `done` once `level >= maxDepth`, and the uniform writer refuses anything that is not an integer in `[0, 2^32 - 1]` (`src/kernel/struct-block.ts` line 94, `E_INVALID_ARGUMENT` naming the field), so the HOST normalises it before any write, to exactly the CPU port's behaviour (`algorithms/src/indexed/bfs.ts` line 43: `d >= maxDepth`, with `INVALID_INDEX` when absent): `normaliseMaxDepth(maxDepth)` is `U32_MAX` when `maxDepth` is absent, `NaN` (`d >= NaN` is never true) or `>= U32_MAX` (which covers `Infinity`), and otherwise `Math.max(0, Math.ceil(maxDepth))` -- `2.5` behaves as 3 because `d >= 2.5` first holds at `d == 3`, and a negative value gives 0, the source alone, because `0 >= -1` already holds. A caller therefore never sees a uniform-field error for a legal `BfsOptions`, and the SSSP unit-weight route of P8-T9 Step 3 hands this function its own derived cap. The `signal` is checked between submits, never inside one.

`RING_SLOTS`, counted rather than guessed, because `UniformRing.reserve` WRAPS to slot 0 when a submit's records outrun the ring (`src/kernel/uniform-ring.ts` line 103) and silently overwrites the submit's first record instead of throwing: per level, `frontier-finalize` twice, `advance-expand`, `bfs-contract` (4 records; P8-T7's two fused dispatches share the contract's record, so still 4 -- built 2026-09-24; P8-T8's `bfs-bitset-build` and `bfs-bottom-up` share it too and its bits `fill` has one record per submit, so still 4 -- built 2026-09-25), plus per submit the P8-T8 rebuild (`bfs-unvisited-flags` and `compact`, whose `lastDispatches` is `2 x scanLevels` -- at most 9 for any `n` below 2^32 -- so 10, plus the bits fill's record, 12), plus the result batch's records (the two `fill`s, `prepareRadixSort`'s per-pass records and `sssp-pred`; they flush in their own submit but must fit the same ring). `RING_SLOTS = 9 * MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT + 16 = 304` covers every submit of the finished driver with room; pin it in `bfs.ts` beside a comment carrying this count, as `pagerank.ts` pins `RING_SLOTS = 2 * PR_BATCH + 1` (line 38), and re-count it in P8-T7 and P8-T8 when they add their records; P8-T12 then turns the pin into `bfsRingSlots(windows, levelsPerSubmit)`, because four of the nine per-level records are re-issued once per arc window (P8-T12 Step 3). The SSSP driver's six records per round (P8-T9 Step 4) fit the same constant. `onProgress(levels so far, n)` fires per submit. `visitedCount`, `levels`, `switches` come from the counters block. `order` costs four stable radix passes over `n` words (about 17 dispatches; sub-millisecond at 1M on the card) and buys a reader-visible number that never changes run to run, which is what graphty-element publishes per node (`BFSAlgorithm.ts` line 152) -- the CPU's FIFO order is deterministic too, but it is a different deterministic order, and design 9.7's "grouped by level" is the parity both satisfy.

`bfsWithTuning`'s `levelsPerSubmit` (default `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT`) and `onLevel` are the `inspect()` seam of design 11.9 item 2 (P8-T15 Step 2): when `onLevel` is given the driver reads the whole 96-byte block and the output frontier at the end of every SUBMIT and hands them to `onLevel` -- at `levelsPerSubmit: 1` that is after every level, which the test compares against the oracle's per-level frontier as a set; at a larger cadence it is the block as the submit left it (P8-T8 Step 5 reads the star's second boundary at cadence 2 this way). Nothing public exposes it. Built 2026-09-24: `onLevel(level, block, frontier)` receives the index of the last level recorded in the submit, the decoded block, and the vertices the submit's last level claimed (`frontier.input` after the last swap, `nextFrontierCount` words), read as two more requests of the SAME batch, so the submit still maps once; the two extra readbacks exist only while `onLevel` is set. P8-T8 adds a fourth argument, the total `compact` wrote when it rebuilt the unvisited list at the top of the submit, as a third request of the same batch. `onProgress(done, n)` reports the levels recorded so far clamped to `n` (a cadence of 32 records past a short traversal). `visitedCount` is checked against `n` before `order` is cut: every vertex is claimed at most once, so a larger count is a kernel bug (a claim that lets two same-level claimants append) and the driver throws `E_VALIDATION { label: "breadthFirstSearch/visitedCount" }` rather than returning it. `direction`, `alpha`, `beta` and `fusedMax` are declared on `BfsTuning` now (the interface line above) but the driver passes `mode 1, fusedMax 0` regardless until P8-T7 (fused) and P8-T8 (Beamer) wire them.

For each, reading the named buffers of Step 4: `depth` EXACT (`toEqual` on the typed array, not a tolerance -- these are `u32`); `expectLevelConsistent` and `expectSmallestPredecessor` on `parent`; `expectOrderGroupedByLevel` on `order` plus set equality per level with the oracle's; `visitedCount` and `levels` equal the oracle's; `maxDepth: 2` on the star and on the grid (`visitedCount` equals the oracle's under the same cap; no depth exceeds 2); the normalisation cases of Step 4 on the grid, each compared with `bfsOracle` called with the SAME raw value and none allowed to throw: `maxDepth: 0` (the source alone, `visitedCount === 1`, `levels === 1`), `maxDepth: 2.5` (identical to `maxDepth: 3`), `maxDepth: -1` (the source alone), `maxDepth: Infinity` and `maxDepth: NaN` (both identical to no cap) -- written BEFORE `normaliseMaxDepth` exists, so the builder watches `Infinity` fail with `UniformBlock: field "maxDepth" is not a u32` first; the run-twice bitwise check on `depth`, `parent`, `order` and the counters block; `validate({ checksum: true })` on the snapshot afterwards (built 2026-09-24: on a snapshot built with `snapshotOf(edges, { checksum: true })`, the freeze option the helper now passes through, since the validation refuses a snapshot frozen without its checksum records; and because a kernel writes DEVICE buffers, which the host-side checksums cannot see, the case also reads the core's `rowPtr` and `colIdx` back from the device and holds them bitwise equal to the snapshot's -- that readback is what proves no kernel wrote into a view). One case proves the predecessor pass strides, independent of the adapter's dispatch cap: bind `sssp-pred` by hand the way `test/kernel/indirect.test.ts` binds `fill` (`ctx.pipelines.kernel(kernelSpec("sssp-pred"))`, `MODE 1`), with `dist` the oracle's depths of `pathEdges(1000)` from source 0 uploaded as a `Uint32Array`, `pred` filled with `INVALID_INDEX`, `P.n = 1000`, `P.stride = WG`, `P.predKind = 1`, `P.source = 0`, and dispatch ONE workgroup (`plan1d(WG, WG, caps)`); read the `pred` buffer back and assert `pred[v] === v - 1` for every `v >= 1` -- rows 256 and up are reached only by the stride.

Then the gate's two named fixtures: `gridEdges(1000, 1000)` scaled by `gpuScale()` (the full 1,000 x 1,000 is P8-T15's `node-limits` case) -- a high-diameter grid whose level count is `w + h - 1`, which is what the `mapAsync` bound is about -- asserting through `test/helpers/leak-counter.ts`, after `await verifyDevice(own); counter.resetMapAsync();` on an adopted device exactly as `pagerank.test.ts` lines 304-313 do, that `counter.mapAsyncCalls === Math.ceil(levels / MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) + 1` (PD-7: one per submit, one for the result batch; exact on every fixture listed here -- 59, 39 and 1,999 levels -- and off by one when `levels` is an exact multiple of 32, because the done boundary is then the 32k+1th and needs one more submit, so a fixture that lands on a multiple asserts `Math.floor(levels / MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) + 2`, which is the same number everywhere else); and the 10,000-degree star from the hub, which puts a single row through the workgroup tier (`bfs-fused`, P8-T7; until then the block-mapped expand strips it in 40 rounds and the assertion is the same depths).

```ts

        { name: "claim-not-a-min", find: "let old = atomicMin(&depth[v], claim);", replace: "let old = atomicExchange(&depth[v], claim);", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "same-level-claimants-append", find: "won = select(0u, 1u, old == INVALID_INDEX);", replace: "won = select(0u, 1u, old >= claim);", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "claim-is-the-level", find: "let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]) + 1u;", replace: "let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]);", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
    ]),
    "sssp-pred": Object.freeze([
        { name: "same-depth-attains", find: "tight = (du + 1u) == dv;", replace: "tight = du == dv;", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "largest-predecessor", find: "atomicMin(&pred[v], select(a, u, P.predKind == 1u));", replace: "atomicMax(&pred[v], select(a, u, P.predKind == 1u));", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "last-row-skipped", find: "for (var u = first; u < P.n; u = u + P.stride) {", replace: "for (var u = first; u + 1u < P.n; u = u + P.stride) {", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },

    ]),
```

The first makes a later level overwrite a smaller depth (`depth` miss); the second appends every same-level claimant (duplicates: `visitedCount` and `order` miss -- built 2026-09-24: on the 900-node grid the duplicates push `visitedCount` to 2,047, above `n`, so the driver refuses the result as `E_VALIDATION` (Step 4) before `order` exists, and `bfsReport` counts that refusal as the `visitedCount` sample at `Infinity`); the third shifts every depth from level 1 on; the fourth breaks level consistency; the fifth fails `expectSmallestPredecessor` on the grid; the sixth fails level consistency from the last-index source on the path; the seventh turns the body back into the per-invocation form, which every traversal fixture under the dispatch cap passes, so its `test` is the one-workgroup case of Step 5, where every row past the first 256 keeps `INVALID_INDEX`. `test/sabotage/bfs.test.ts` runs the rows plus P8-T4's `rotation-dropped` on `gridEdges(30, 30)` (from the corner) and `pathEdges(500)` (from its LAST index: the sixth row survives every other source) through `bfsReport` (`test/helpers/bfs.ts`), which compares `depth`, `parent` (against the host's smallest-predecessor rule), `order` (against the host's (depth, index) sort), `visitedCount` and `levels` bitwise and adds the one-workgroup `pred` words; every one reports a bitwise miss. - [ ] **Step 2: The fused body -- the workgroup-per-row tier with the claim inline**

One workgroup per frontier entry, every lane striding that entry's row, the claim of P8-T6 applied inline instead of appending to an edge queue -- one dispatch, no edge queue traffic, which is the whole win for a tiny frontier, and the "workgroup-per-row tier" of design 6 row 8 (P8-T5 Step 3). The strip loop's bound is a `workgroupUniformLoad`, so the barriers of the per-strip append are in uniform control flow; the plain body has two barriers per strip plus the scan's, and a trailing barrier before `sh` and `base` are reused:

```ts
export const bfsFusedWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
var<workgroup> sh: array<u32, WG>;
var<workgroup> wdeg: u32;
var<workgroup> wstart: u32;
var<workgroup> base: u32;

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bfs_fused(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let g = group_id(wid);                                           // one workgroup per frontier entry
    let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]) + 1u;
    if (lid.x == 0u) {
        var d = 0u;
        var a0 = 0u;
        if (g < atomicLoad(&counters[0])) {
            let u = frontierIn[g];
            a0 = max(rowPtr[u], P.arcBase);
            let a1 = min(rowPtr[u + 1u], P.arcEnd);
            d = select(0u, a1 - a0, a1 > a0);
        }
        wdeg = d;
        wstart = a0;
        atomicAdd(&counters[2], d);                                  // frontierDegreeSum, so Beamer's test (P8-T8) sees fused levels too
    }
    let deg = workgroupUniformLoad(&wdeg);                           // uniform: the loop below may hold barriers
    let start = workgroupUniformLoad(&wstart);
    for (var p0 = 0u; p0 < deg; p0 = p0 + WG) {                      // strip the row WG arcs at a time
        let p = p0 + lid.x;
        var won = 0u;
        var v = 0u;
        if (p < deg) {                                               // guarded claim into locals
            v = colIdx[start + p - P.arcBase];
            let old = atomicMin(&depth[v], claim);
            won = select(0u, 1u, old == INVALID_INDEX);
        }
        sh[lid.x] = won;
        workgroupBarrier();
        for (var s = 1u; s < WG; s = s * 2u) {                       // Hillis-Steele inclusive scan of won (bfs-contract's, verbatim)
            var t = 0u;
            if (lid.x >= s) { t = sh[lid.x - s]; }
            workgroupBarrier();
            sh[lid.x] = sh[lid.x] + t;
            workgroupBarrier();
        }
        let inclusive = sh[lid.x];
        if (lid.x == WG - 1u) { base = atomicAdd(&counters[1], inclusive); }
        workgroupBarrier();
        if (won == 1u) { frontierOut[base + inclusive - 1u] = v; }
        workgroupBarrier();                                          // sh and base are reused by the next strip
    }
}
`;
```

Keep the claim line and the scan textually identical to `bfs-contract`'s so a reader can diff them. Registry: the four graph slots, `(1,0) frontierIn ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) depth rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,3) frontierOut rw array<u32>`, `(2,0) P`; storage count 8 -- at the budget, which is why no `parent` lives here (PD-24).

- [ ] **Step 3: The selector branch and the driver.** P8-T4's body already holds `else if (next < P.fusedMax)`; the driver now passes `fusedMax = tuning.fusedMax ?? FUSED_FRONTIER_MAX` instead of 0, records `bfs-fused` twice per level -- `dispatchIndirect(..., SLOT.fused)` and `dispatchIndirect(..., SLOT.fusedRetry)`, the same bound kernel -- after the contract, and the level sequence becomes: finalize 0, expand, finalize 1, contract, fused, fused-retry, swap. `FUSED_MAX` is a uniform field, not a compile-time constant, so a test can set it to 0 or to `U32_MAX` and force either path without recompiling. Built 2026-09-24: both fused dispatches share the contract's `FrontierParams` record (one `scope.params` per level, now carrying `arcBase` / `arcEnd` too, which the contract ignores), so a level is still FOUR ring records, not six, and `RING_SLOTS` is unchanged; P8-T12's per-window re-issue is where the fused dispatch gets a record of its own. On an overflow level the retry runs `bfs-fused` over the frontier `advance-expand` already expanded, and both add the frontier's degree to `frontierDegreeSum` (word 2), so that word doubles on a retry level and the next boundary's Beamer test and degree-sum subtraction see `2 x m_f`; reachable only with a faked capacity or an absurd graph, so it is accepted and said in the kernel's JSDoc rather than guarded.

- [ ] **Step 4: Both paths agree, and the selection is pinned to counters.** Run the whole P8-T6 suite three times through `bfsWithTuning`: `fusedMax 0` (never fused), `fusedMax U32_MAX` (always fused), and the default. All three produce identical `depth`, `parent` and `order` (bitwise, PD-14). Then, on `rmatEdges(14, 10, 7)` at the default, read the counters block back with the result and assert BOTH paths were chosen: `fusedLevels > 0` and `twoPhaseLevels > 0` -- design 13's gate item "the device-side selection picks each at least once on an RMAT fixture" -- and, exactly, that `fusedLevels` equals the number of levels whose oracle frontier size is below 4,096 and `twoPhaseLevels` the number at or above it (the oracle's `depth` gives every level's size; level 0's size is 1). Record which levels chose which in the gate record. Then the gate's overflow item (PD-23), which needs the two-phase path FORCED, because with the default `fusedMax` a one-entry frontier (the star's hub) takes the fused path and never fills the edge queue: `bfsWithTuning({ edgeCapacity: 4096, fusedMax: 0 })` on `starEdges(10000)` from the hub and on `rmatEdges(14, 10, 7)`: `depth` exact, `overflowLevels > 0` -- exactly 2 on the UNDIRECTED star, because both of its levels put more than 4,096 arcs into the queue (10,000 out of the hub at level 0, 10,000 back from the leaves at level 1; the directed star from the hub gives 1, and the test asserts both) -- and `fusedLevels + twoPhaseLevels + bottomUpLevels === counters.level`, the number of boundaries that dispatched a level (`levels` when the frontier emptied, `levels - 1` when `maxDepth` stopped the run, since the capped last level chose nothing). That invariant holds because a two-phase level counts when the contract slot is CHOSEN, even for zero edges (P8-T4 Step 6's role 1, which reads role 0's choice from slot 0 and counts nothing on a fused, bottom-up or done boundary); a sink level in a directed fixture or an isolated source is therefore counted, not skipped.

```ts

        { name: "claim-outside-the-guard", find: "if (p < deg) {                                               // guarded claim into locals", replace: "if (true) {", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "fused-scan-round-dropped", find: "for (var s = 1u; s < WG; s = s * 2u) {                       // Hillis-Steele inclusive scan of won (bfs-contract's, verbatim)", replace: "for (var s = 1u; s < WG; s = s * 4u) {", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
        { name: "fused-claim-is-the-level", find: "let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]) + 1u;\n    if (lid.x == 0u) {", replace: "let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]);\n    if (lid.x == 0u) {", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST },
    ]),
```

and on `frontier-finalize`: `{ name: "fused-threshold-inverted", find: "} else if (next < P.fusedMax) {", replace: "} else if (next >= P.fusedMax) {", minFactor: 10, test: BFS_TEST }`. The first claims past the row's end (the robustness clamp makes it a claim on the wrong vertex); the second breaks the appends; the third shifts every fused depth; the fourth leaves every depth right and is caught ONLY by Step 4's exact `fusedLevels` count against the oracle's level sizes -- a counter assertion, never a timing. The `find` strings of the first three occur once each in the fused body (the scan comment differs from `bfs-contract`'s on purpose). Watch each go red.

Expected: PASS; the "each path chosen at least once" assertion green on the RMAT fixture. Measured 2026-09-24 (with `test/sabotage/frontier.test.ts` added to the Run line): every row `Infinity` on lavapipe and on the RTX 4070 SUPER with identical labels -- `claim-outside-the-guard` and `fused-scan-round-dropped` at `grid30/fused.depth[2]`, `fused-claim-is-the-level` at `grid30/fused.depth[1]`, `fused-threshold-inverted` at `rmat14.fusedLevels`, and P8-T4's `fused-slot-per-invocation`, measured here as well, at `grid30/fused.depth[60]`; the body compiled on Dawn 0.4.0 at the first attempt on both adapters. The levels each path took, for the gate record: `rmatEdges(14, 10, 7)` from 0 has level sizes 1, 2904, 8125, 459, 2 undirected and 1, 1857, 7101, 829, 20 directed, so at the default threshold the selector fuses four levels and runs one two-phase in both cases; with `edgeCapacity 4096, fusedMax 0` the undirected star from the hub overflows twice, the directed star once, and rmat14 at least once, `fusedLevels` equalling `overflowLevels` throughout. The 18-file run (593 tests) takes 20 s on lavapipe and 64 s on the card at load average 25.

### Task P8-T8: Direction-optimizing BFS -- the unvisited set, the bitset, the bottom-up sweep, Beamer's test

**Depends on P8-T7, and on P8-T3's `compact`. Uses P7's `reverse()` residency and the `outDegree` / `inDegree` views.**

**Spec:** design 8.4 (Beamer's constants, the switch conditions, the bitset frontier, the non-zero-degree unvisited list the sweep iterates, and the rule that the switch is evaluated on the device from counters the advance already produces); design 8.10 "BFS bottom-up" (8 bindings).

**Files:** Create `$PKG/src/wgsl/bfs-bottom-up.wgsl.ts`, `$PKG/src/wgsl/bfs-bitset-build.wgsl.ts`, `$PKG/src/wgsl/bfs-unvisited-flags.wgsl.ts`; modify `$PKG/src/kernels.ts` (three entries), `$PKG/src/wgsl/frontier-finalize.wgsl.ts` (the `direction` line), `$PKG/src/algorithms/bfs.ts`, `$PKG/test/kernel/bind-group-budget.test.ts` (`"bfs-bottom-up": 8, "bfs-bitset-build": 3, "bfs-unvisited-flags": 5`), `$PKG/test/kernel/registry.test.ts`, `$PKG/test/kernel/wgsl-compile.test.ts` (pins 5, 1, 1), `$PKG/test/helpers/override-matrix.ts`, `$PKG/test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts`, `$PKG/test/sabotage/bfs.test.ts`, `$PKG/test/helpers/sabotage.ts`; and (built 2026-09-25, absent from the 2026-09-23 list) `$PKG/test/helpers/traversal-check.ts` (`expectedDirections`, the host model Step 5 names), `$PKG/test/helpers/bfs.ts` (`directionModel`, `unvisitedListLenAt`, the hub-clique fixture, `directionReports`, and `bfsReport`'s new runs), `$PKG/test/sabotage/frontier.test.ts` (the eight-name list, two rows naming the BFS test), and `$PKG/src/primitives/core-shape.ts` (`coreOfView` maps a ZERO-SIZE `colIdx` / `weights` binding to null: the reverse view of an arc-less DIRECTED snapshot uploads zero-byte arc arrays, and the sweep's `graphBindings` would otherwise bind a zero-length range, which `Kernel.bind` refuses -- the one-node directed fixture of the BFS suite is where it showed). NOT touched: `$PKG/src/primitives/compact.ts` -- this task CALLS `prepareCompact` from P8-T3 and adds nothing to it. Consumes `coreOfView` (`src/primitives/core-shape.ts` line 155) for the reverse core, `residency.view(s, "reverse")`, `view(s, "outDegree")`, `view(s, "inDegree")`, the prelude's `mask_bit` and `wg_reduce_u32` (with `combine_u`'s sum code), `SLOT.fillBits` / `SLOT.bitset` / `SLOT.bottomUp`, `W.*`.

Expected: FAIL with `AssertionError: expected 0 to be greater than 0` on `switches`. Built 2026-09-25: exactly that (`switches (the counters block) at the default: expected 0 to be greater than 0`), with the model helper of Step 5 in place first (it is test code, not the implementation).

- [ ] **Step 2: The unvisited set, which is the thing Beamer's test is against (PD-18)**

A bottom-up level sweeps the vertices that have NOT been reached, so this task must first produce four things that nothing earlier in the phase produces: `unvisitedList` (the unvisited vertices with a non-zero in-degree -- what the sweep iterates), `unvisitedListLen` (word 7, what sizes the sweep's dispatch), `unvisitedDegreeSum` (word 6, the out-degree sum of the unvisited vertices, Beamer's `m_u`) and `unvisitedCount` (word 5). They are produced two ways and both are needed.

_Exactly, once per submit._ `bfs-unvisited-flags` grid-strides over the vertices (`planGridStride(n)`; `P.stride` is the plan's stride):

```ts
export const bfsUnvisitedFlagsWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bfs_unvisited_flags(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let first = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    var cnt = 0u;
    var degSum = 0u;
    var len = 0u;
    for (var v = first; v < P.n; v = v + P.stride) {                 // no barrier inside: the trip count is per lane
        let unv = depth[v] == INVALID_INDEX;
        let listed = unv && (inDegree[v] != 0u);
        flags[v] = select(0u, 1u, listed);
        cnt = cnt + select(0u, 1u, unv);
        degSum = degSum + select(0u, outDegree[v], unv);             // the OUT-degree: Beamer's m_u counts the edges top-down would examine
        len = len + select(0u, 1u, listed);
    }
    let c = wg_reduce_u32(cnt, lid.x, 0u);                           // the prelude's workgroup sum (combine_u's sum code); uniform: after the loop
    let d = wg_reduce_u32(degSum, lid.x, 0u);
    let l = wg_reduce_u32(len, lid.x, 0u);
    if (lid.x == 0u) {                                               // ONE atomic per word per workgroup
        atomicAdd(&counters[5], c);
        atomicAdd(&counters[6], d);
        atomicAdd(&counters[7], l);
    }
}
`;
```

(`wg_reduce_u32`'s op code is `combine_u`'s sum code in `src/kernel/prelude.ts`; the builder reads it there rather than trusting the `0u` above.) Bindings: `(1,0) outDegree ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) inDegree ro array<u32>`, `(1,2) depth ro array<u32>`, `(1,3) flags rw array<u32>`, `(1,4) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(2,0) P`; storage count 5; `needs: ["subgroups"]`, because the body calls `wg_reduce_u32` and the composer refuses the call without it (P8-T1 Step 5) -- a twin kernel; the two degree VIEWS rather than the graph group, because in-degree needs the reverse row pointer and the view is the same upload on an undirected snapshot. Then `prepareCompact.record(pass, { queue: iota, flags, count: n, out: sweepIn[0 .. n), outCount: compactCount, outIndex: 0 })` turns `flags` into `unvisitedList` -- `queue` is an `iota` scratch (`fill` mode 1, once at setup) so `compact` needs no identity special case -- and `compactCount` is a one-word scratch that Step 5 compares with word 7. The three words are zeroed by `queue.writeBuffer` (12 bytes at offset 20) before the submit. `flags`, `sweepIn` (`roundUp(n, 64) + ceil(n / 32)` words: `unvisitedList` at word 0, `frontierBits` at word `bitsBase = roundUp(n, 64)`, so the bits region's byte offset is 256-aligned and `fill` can bind it alone), `iota` and `compactCount` are taken from the algorithm's lease, so they are released with it, and the `Frontier` class of P8-T4 does not change. Built 2026-09-25: `compactCount` reaches the tests as the FOURTH argument of `onLevel(level, block, frontier, compactCount)`, read as a third request of the same submit batch while `onLevel` is set, because nothing else can see a scratch word of the driver's lease.

The host records this rebuild ONCE at the top of every submit, before the levels, unconditionally -- never per level, never gated on the direction, so it needs no candidate slot and costs one pass over `n` per 32 levels. On the 1000 x 1000 grid that is about 63 rebuilds across a 1,999-level traversal; on an RMAT graph of diameter 10 it is one. Which boundaries of a submit subtract is fixed by what the rebuild counted, and the two words differ by one boundary. Number the boundaries of a submit `b = 0, 1, 2, ...`; the driver passes `Math.min(b, 2)` as `P.firstOfSubmit` and the guards are in P8-T4's body. The rebuild counts the vertices still unclaimed when it runs; the frontier rotated in at `b = 0` was claimed by the previous submit's last contract (for the first submit it is the source, whose depth the host wrote before the rebuild), so it was never in the sum. Boundary `b` subtracts `next = |F_b|`, claimed by the contract of level `b - 1`, which is inside the rebuilt sum iff `b >= 1`; and `degSum = deg(F_{b-1})`, the frontier expanded at level `b - 1` and rotated in at boundary `b - 1`, which is inside the rebuilt sum iff `b >= 2`. So the count subtraction runs from the second boundary of a submit on and the degree-sum subtraction from the third on. A body that subtracts the degree sum from the second boundary understates the word by `deg(F_0)` for the rest of the submit -- on the ten-leaf star from the hub the word reads 0 after the second boundary where the next paragraph promises 10, on a grid every later boundary is low by `deg(F_0)` -- which makes the switch INTO bottom-up fire EARLIER than the rule intends and wraps the u32 below zero whenever `deg(F_0)` exceeds what remains; the boundary-2 star assertion of Step 5 is the test that sees it.

_By subtraction, at every level in between._ Inside a submit the unvisited set only shrinks, and `frontier-finalize` already holds the numbers that say by how much: in its level-boundary role it does `unvisitedCount -= next` (exact: the vertices just claimed) and `unvisitedDegreeSum -= frontierDegreeSum` (the degree sum of the frontier just EXPANDED, which `advance-expand` or `bfs-fused` measured while expanding it). Two things make the degree sum the "unvisited degree estimate" the design calls it rather than an exact count: it is one level stale, because a frontier's degree sum is only known once it has been expanded; and a bottom-up level expands nothing, so `unvisitedDegreeSum` stops falling while bottom-up runs and overstates the set afterwards. The bias is one-directional -- an overstated `m_u` makes the switch INTO bottom-up harder, never easier -- and the next submit's rebuild makes it exact again. Say that in the kernel's JSDoc, with this paragraph's reason, and state the boundary rule there too, in these words: the count is subtracted from the second boundary of a submit on and the degree sum from the third on, because a boundary may only subtract what the submit's rebuild counted, and the frontier whose degree sum the second boundary holds was claimed before the rebuild ran.

Because the list is up to 32 levels stale it holds vertices that have since been claimed. The sweep skips them on the `depth[v] == INVALID_INDEX` test it has to make anyway, so staleness costs a few wasted reads and can never produce a wrong depth.

- [ ] **Step 3: The bitset and the hand-off.** ONE `ceil(n / 32)`-word bitset, rebuilt per level rather than ping-ponged, zeroed by a `fill` over the bits region (its own indirect slot, `SLOT.fillBits`, so a top-down level skips it) and then written by `bfs-bitset-build`:

```ts
export const bfsBitsetBuildWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bfs_bitset_build(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let i = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    if (i >= atomicLoad(&counters[0])) { return; }                   // frontierCount; no barrier follows
    let v = frontierIn[i];
    atomicOr(&bits[P.bitsBase + (v >> 5u)], 1u << (v & 31u));
}
`;
```

Bindings `(1,0) frontierIn ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) bits rw array<atomic<u32>>` (the whole `sweepIn` buffer; `P.bitsBase` addresses the region), storage count 3; `needs: []`. The design's "bulk non-atomic path when the frontier is >= 40% of n" iterates WORDS of a frontier that is already a bitset; this frontier is a vertex list, so a word-owning store has nothing to iterate and `atomicOr` per vertex is the whole kernel -- one sentence in the JSDoc. The bottom-up sweep appends the vertices it claims to `frontierOut` as a plain vertex list, exactly as the contract phase does, and writes NO second bitset: the next level's bits come from `bfs-bitset-build` running over that list, which is a recorded candidate of every level anyway. That leaves one representation of a frontier in the whole phase -- a vertex list plus a count -- makes the bottom-up-to-top-down hand-off free, and is what keeps the sweep inside eight storage buffers with the four graph slots.

- [ ] **Step 4: The bottom-up sweep.** Over `reverse()` (`coreOfView(residency.view(s, "reverse"), arcCount)` -- the forward arrays when the snapshot is undirected, which P7's residency already aliases at zero upload cost), one invocation per entry of `unvisitedList[0 .. unvisitedListLen)`:

```ts
export const bfsBottomUpWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
var<workgroup> sh: array<u32, WG>;
var<workgroup> base: u32;

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bfs_bottom_up(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let i = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    let len = atomicLoad(&counters[7]);                              // unvisitedListLen
    let claim = atomicLoad(&counters[11]) + 1u;
    var won = 0u;
    var v = 0u;
    var reads = 0u;
    if (i < len) {                                                   // guarded work into locals
        v = sweepIn[i];
        if (atomicLoad(&depth[v]) == INVALID_INDEX) {                // a stale entry, claimed since the rebuild, is skipped
            let end = min(rowPtr[v + 1u], P.arcEnd);
            for (var a = max(rowPtr[v], P.arcBase); a < end; a = a + 1u) {   // in-neighbours through the reverse core
                reads = reads + 1u;
                let u = colIdx[a - P.arcBase];
                if (mask_bit(sweepIn[P.bitsBase + (u >> 5u)], u)) { won = 1u; break; }   // the early exit: a real break, never a flag
            }
        }
    }
    sh[lid.x] = won;
    workgroupBarrier();
    for (var s = 1u; s < WG; s = s * 2u) {                           // Hillis-Steele inclusive scan of won (bfs-contract's)
        var t = 0u;
        if (lid.x >= s) { t = sh[lid.x - s]; }
        workgroupBarrier();
        sh[lid.x] = sh[lid.x] + t;
        workgroupBarrier();
    }
    let inclusive = sh[lid.x];
    let readsTotal = wg_reduce_u32(reads, lid.x, 0u);                // arcsScanned, one atomic per workgroup (the sabotage witness, Step 6)
    if (lid.x == WG - 1u) { base = atomicAdd(&counters[1], inclusive); }
    if (lid.x == 0u) { atomicAdd(&counters[16], readsTotal); }
    workgroupBarrier();
    if (won == 1u) {
        atomicStore(&depth[v], claim);                               // no claim race: the list holds v once and the sweep is vertex-parallel
        frontierOut[base + inclusive - 1u] = v;
    }
}
`;
```

Bindings: the four graph slots of the REVERSE core, `(1,0) sweepIn ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) depth rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,3) frontierOut rw array<u32>`, `(2,0) P`; storage count 8; `needs: ["subgroups"]` (the `wg_reduce_u32` call for `arcsScanned`; a twin kernel, P8-T1 Step 5). The sweep binds `sweepIn` read-only while `bfs-bitset-build` and `compact` bind the same buffer read-write in OTHER dispatches, which `Kernel.bind` allows (its access-mode check is per dispatch). Built 2026-09-25: the bitset build and the sweep SHARE the contract's `FrontierParams` record (it gained `bitsBase`; the five consumers read the same `wg`, `n`, `edgeCapacity` and arc window), and the bits `fill` has ONE `FillParams` record per submit shared by its 32 levels, so a level is still FOUR ring records and a level submit holds `4 x 32 + 12` (the fill, the flags kernel and `compact`'s at most ten); `RING_SLOTS = 9 * MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT + 16` is unchanged and its comment in `bfs.ts` carries this count. P8-T12's per-window re-issue must give the sweep its own record if the reverse core is ever windowed differently from the forward one.

- [ ] **Step 5: The switch, on the device (PD-21), and the proof.** In `frontier-finalize`'s role 0, the `var direction = 0u;` line of P8-T4 becomes:

```
    var direction = atomicLoad(&counters[14]);
    if (P.mode == 1u) {
        direction = 0u;                                                 // top-down only (the test seam)
    } else if (direction == 0u) {
        if (degSum > atomicLoad(&counters[6]) / P.alpha && next > finished) { direction = 1u; }   // m_f > m_u / alpha and growing
    } else {
        if (next * P.beta < atomicLoad(&counters[5]) && next < finished) { direction = 0u; }      // next * beta < unvisited and shrinking
    }
    if (direction != atomicLoad(&counters[14])) { atomicStore(&counters[13], atomicLoad(&counters[13]) + 1u); }   // switches
```

`alpha = max(1, floor(arcCount / nodeCount))` is computed on the host and passed as the `alpha` uniform (`tuning.alpha` overrides it); `beta = BEAMER_BETA` (`tuning.beta` overrides). Why the derived alpha is the design's rule and why there is no constant: with `alpha = m / n` the test reads "the frontier's edges exceed the unvisited edges divided by the average degree", which is about the number of unvisited VERTICES bottom-up would have to touch. On a sparse graph whose arc-to-node ratio is 2 -- a path or a cycle, undirected -- alpha is 2 and bottom-up is entered only when the frontier's edges exceed HALF the unvisited edges, which on a path is the last two levels; Beamer's published 14 would enter it seven times earlier, on a graph where a bottom-up level costs a read of every unvisited vertex for a frontier of one, and that is the wrong direction to err in. On an RMAT graph at edge factor 10 (ratio 20 undirected) the derived value is 20 and the switch fires at the peak levels, which Step 5's model shows. `next * P.beta` is a u32 product; it wraps above 178M vertices, and `n` is below that on every device this package admits, so the wrap is noted, not guarded. The words the test reads are all in the block, and P8-T4 Step 3's table names the kernel that writes each.

**Amendment 2026-09-25 (issue #391, branch fix/webgpu-bfs-direction-rule): m_f is measured on the frontier the boundary decides FOR, not on the one just expanded.** As built above, the boundary's `degSum` was `frontierDegreeSum` (word 2), which `advance-expand` / `bfs-fused` accumulate while EXPANDING the previous frontier, so the switch-into-bottom-up test compared deg(F_{L-1}) with the unvisited set at boundary L -- one level stale, and 0 after a bottom-up level (the sweep expands nothing), which also left `unvisitedDegreeSum` un-subtracted through every bottom-up level until the next rebuild. On the 1M / 10M R-MAT (2^20 x 10, undirected, source 0) that is the difference between the two rules: a CPU replay over the oracle's levels (tmp/bfs-sweep/sweep.ts on the branch) gives level 1 a frontier of 46,524 hubs with 13,574,685 out-arcs against 7,310,430 unvisited arcs, so the exact test switches there (13.6M > 7.3M / 20) and the sweep reads 593,897 arcs where the top-down expansion writes 13.6M edge-queue entries; the stale rule saw the source's 86,405 and stayed top-down until level 2. Per level the bottom-up sweep is cheaper at levels 1, 2 and 3 (0.04x, 0.01x, 0.02x of the top-down reads) and top-down at 0, 4 and 5, and the exact rule picks exactly that sequence (bottom-up 3 levels, 649,743 sweep reads, 2 switches); at 100k / 1M (2^17 x 8) the same: bottom-up at levels 1-3, 80,781 reads against 1.4M + 0.65M + 7.6k. The fix is one kernel, `bfs-next-degree`, dispatched at the end of every level over the output vertex queue: it sums the `outDegree` view over the vertices the level claimed (whichever path claimed them) into a new word 25, `nextDegreeSum`, and role 0 of `frontier-finalize` reads that word as m_f, subtracts it from `unvisitedDegreeSum` from the SECOND boundary of a submit on -- the same rule as the count, since F_b was claimed inside the submit iff b >= 1 -- and zeroes it. Both unvisited words are therefore exact at every boundary; the "one-level staleness the design accepts" above and the "third boundary" rule for the degree sum are superseded, and `expectedDirections` models the exact rule (degSum = deg(F_level), both words subtracted at b >= 1). Word 2 keeps its expansion sense for the inspect seam and the overflow-retry test. The alpha / beta sweep the issue asked for (alpha in {10, 20, 40, 80, 160, 320} x beta in {24, 48, 96}, medians of 3 warm calls with the core resident, load average 8-16) moved nothing within noise on either R-MAT rung until alpha reached the value that forces the level-1 switch (320 at 1M / 10M, 160 at 100k / 1M), which is the exact rule's choice; the grid never switches under any value, as before. The issue's premise -- auto slower than top-down on the 1M / 10M row -- did not reproduce on the traversal itself: resident, auto measured 32-38 ms against top-down 33-41 ms before the fix; the 156.6 against 88.6 ms of the issue was the benchmark row's upload plus the card falling to P5 / 1.5 GHz during the auto block (`tmp/bfs-sweep/row.ts`), so the alpha / beta constants stay as PD-21 fixed them.

Proof, every assertion on a counter word read from the block: `bfsWithTuning({ direction: "top-down" })` disables the bottom-up candidate; run the whole suite in both modes and assert identical `depth`, `parent`, `order`. On `rmatEdges(16, 10, 3)` assert `switches > 0` at the default and `switches === 0` under `"top-down"` -- design 13's gate item -- and, exactly, two things against a twenty-line host model of Beamer's rule that replays the selector from the oracle's per-level frontier sizes and degree sums with the same one-level staleness and the same per-submit rebuild (`expectedDirections(levelSizes, levelDegreeSums, n, arcCount, alpha, beta, levelsPerSubmit)` in `test/helpers/traversal-check.ts`; the model rebuilds both unvisited words exactly from the oracle's complement at the top of every submit, subtracts the count from the second boundary of a submit on and the degree sum from the third on -- Step 2's rule, restated here so the model cannot mirror a wrong body -- and evaluates the rule at the `done` boundary too, because the kernel's test replaces the `var direction = 0u;` line, which sits BEFORE the `if (done)` branch, so a switch can be counted there): at the production cadence (`levelsPerSubmit` 32, no `onLevel`) the result's `switches` equals the model's count, and at `levelsPerSubmit: 1` (where `onLevel` hands back the block after every level, and the model is run with 1 so the rebuild cadence matches) the per-level `direction` words equal the model's sequence. Add the RMAT fixture to the run-twice check: `switches` is a device counter over a deterministic rule, so it is reproducible and must be asserted as such. Built 2026-09-25: `expectedDirections` takes two more optional arguments, `maxDepth` (raw, the CPU rule `level >= maxDepth`, so the model runs under a cap) and `topDown` (the driver's `mode 1`), and returns one record per boundary (`direction`, `unvisitedCount`, `unvisitedDegreeSum`, `switches`, `done`), the last one `done`, so a test at cadence c compares the block after submit k with boundary `min(last, (k + 1) c - 1)`; `directionModel(s, depth, tuning, maxDepth)` in `test/helpers/bfs.ts` derives the level sizes and out-degree sums from the oracle's depth and the alpha, beta, cadence and mode from the tuning. The fixture loop's `checkRun` holds EVERY fixture's `switches` equal to the model's at the production cadence (not `0`: under the derived alpha the 10,000-leaf star switches once from a leaf undirected and once from the hub directed, both as the model says) and runs every fixture once more under `direction: "top-down"` (switches 0, the three arrays bitwise identical) beside the two forced thresholds; the RMAT gate case runs rmat16 at the default twice (switches equal, the last block equal), under top-down, and at cadence 1 against the model's sequence. P8-T7's choice-counter and overflow cases and `bfsReport`'s forced-path and choice runs are pinned to `direction: "top-down"`, because under the default rule rmat14 goes bottom-up at level 2 (its fused / two-phase counts are then not the threshold's alone) and a retry level's doubled `frontierDegreeSum` flips the test on the star (the undirected star would then overflow once, not twice).
