- [ ] **Step 2: PD-9 -- distances as bit patterns, and why the result is bitwise reproducible**

`dist` is `array<atomic<u32>>` holding the IEEE-754 bit patterns of the f32 distances, `F32_INF_BITS` (`+Inf`) for unreached, filled by `fill` and `dist[source] = 0` by `queue.writeBuffer`. `atomicMin` on the bit patterns is exactly `min` on the values, because for non-negative IEEE-754 floats the unsigned bit-pattern order is the value order: `+0` is `0x00000000`, every sum of non-negatives under round-to-nearest is `+0` or positive (never `-0`, `0x80000000`), and `flags.nonNegativeWeights` (or the host scan of `options.weights`) is what guarantees the operands. That is the whole trick and it needs no float atomic, which WGSL does not have.

The consequence changes how this task is TESTED: every candidate value is one f32 add, `dist[u] + w`; the near-far loop keeps relaxing until both piles are empty, so every path is eventually offered; and the settled value is the minimum of a fixed set of f32 numbers. A minimum is order-independent. So `dist` is bitwise reproducible run to run, bitwise identical across adapters, and -- PD-10 -- bitwise equal to the f32 Dijkstra oracle of P8-T2, PROVIDED the device's f32 add is correctly rounded, which `Math.fround(a + b)` on two f32 operands is (an f64 sum of two f32 values is exact to 2p + 2 bits, so the double rounding is innocuous). The differential test compares `dist` with `toEqual` on a `Uint32Array` view of both, not with a tolerance. The f64 oracle is compared with a tolerance, and that tolerance is DERIVED: it is whatever spread P8-T2 Step 4 measured between f32 and f64 on the same fixture, times ten, recorded in `benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json` and read through `noiseFloorFor("sssp-relax.f32-vs-f64")` -- built 2026-09-25: recorded by THIS task, not P8-T15, because the differential cannot be green before the id exists: the suite's own case measures the spread of the f32 oracle against the f64 one over every fixture and source of its list (6.422e-7 relative, 7.0 absolute, over 76,447 finite distances; the 500-hop path is the worst) and records the row under `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1` (comparison `oracle-f64`, `a` `oracle-f32`, `b` `oracle-f64`), and the tolerance was derived by the rule of `test/noise-floor.test.ts` (`min(cap, 10 x max(floor, 2^-24))`, factor 10): 6.422e-6 under the design 9.7 cap of 1e-5, so no owner decision is needed; `ssspTolerance()` returns the cap during a recording run and the committed value otherwise, `frTolerance`'s shape. P8-T15 Step 6 keeps the cross-adapter and twin rows. On a 2,000-hop path the f32 drift is around `1e-4` relative, which is looser than design 9.7's `1e-5`; if the measurement confirms that, the tolerance is re-fixed by a recorded owner decision in the pull request and never quietly loosened in the test file (design 10.4's rule, restated in 11.9 item 3). If a device is found whose add is not correctly rounded, the f32 comparison drops to the derived tolerance and the finding goes in G8 (risk RP-3); nothing else changes.

- [ ] **Step 3: The two routings and the weight vector, checked before any device work (PD-22)**

The run's weight vector is `options.weights` when given (a `NumericVector`, `arcCount` long or `E_INVALID_ARGUMENT { argument: "weights" }`; narrowed to a `Float32Array` when it is not one -- the driver's JSDoc beside that line says what the narrowing costs: a `U32` or `I32` value above 2^24, or the bits of an `F64` value below the f32 ulp, is rounded silently, the package-wide f32 caveat and not a refusal -- uploaded into a lease buffer, and bound in the graph group's `weights` slot through `graphBindings(core, null, binding)` with the pipeline compiled through `graphOverrides(core, null, binding)`, so `HAS_WEIGHTS` is true even on an unweighted snapshot), else the snapshot's column. The flags of the run are the snapshot's `s.flags` for the column and a one-pass host scan of the override otherwise (`allOne`, `nonNegative`, `finite`, `sum`): `cutoff` is normalised on the host FIRST, on both routes, before any device work: absent means no cap; `NaN` is `E_INVALID_ARGUMENT { argument: "cutoff" }` (the CPU port's `dv <= NaN` relaxes nothing while the kernel's `nd > NaN` guard would relax everything -- a silently inverted option, so it is refused rather than mirrored); anything else is a finite or infinite number. `allWeightsOne` (or no weight vector at all) routes to `bfsWithTuning` with a depth cap derived from it -- no cap or `+Infinity` gives `maxDepth U32_MAX`, a negative cutoff gives `maxDepth 0` (the source alone: `dv <= -1` never holds), and otherwise `maxDepth = Math.floor(cutoff)`, which then goes through P8-T6 Step 4's `normaliseMaxDepth` like every other cap, so the element's plain `dispatch.sssp(s, sourceIndex)` on an unweighted graph (`DijkstraAlgorithm.ts` line 152) never hands `Infinity` to a `u32` field (unit weights make `dv <= cutoff` the depth bound `depth <= floor(cutoff)`) -- and converts: `dist[v] = depth[v]` as f32, `+Inf` where `INVALID_INDEX`, `predArc` from `sssp-pred` in `MODE 1` with `predKind 0` (the smallest arc into `v` from depth `depth[v] - 1`; acyclic because depth strictly decreases along it, PD-27) -- through `bfsWithTuning({ predKind: 0 })`, P8-T6's tuning field, because the `depth` buffer the pass reads lives in that driver's scope and is released when it returns --, `reachedCount = visitedCount` -- not a fallback, a better algorithm for the input; `nonNegativeWeights === false` throws `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "sssp.negativeWeights", hint: "use bellmanFord" }`; `finiteWeights === false` throws `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "sssp.nonFiniteWeights" }` (the bit-pattern order is undefined for NaN). All host-side before the first dispatch, all tested. `delta = SSSP_DELTA_FACTOR * avgWeight / avgDegree` with `avgWeight = sum / arcCount` (the f64 sum of the vector in use) and `avgDegree = arcCount / n`; it is written into the counters block as `deltaBits` and `thresholdBits` (the first bucket is `[0, delta)`), `prevThresholdBits` (word 4, unused by BFS) as `0`; `tuning.delta` overrides it for tests. A zero delta (an all-zero vector: `sum === 0`) would land every relaxation on the far pile and never grow the threshold, so `delta = sum === 0 ? 1 : ...` (every distance is then 0, inside the first bucket). A RUN with no weight vector -- no weight column AND no `options.weights` override -- never reaches this line: it is `allWeightsOne` by definition. A snapshot with no column but an override that is not all ones DOES reach it: the routing is by the run's flags (the host scan of the override), never by the snapshot's `s.flags` alone, because the seam's JSDoc names exactly that call (the facade passes `expandEdges(s, shadow.data)` as `weights`, `algorithms/src/indexed/dijkstra.ts` line 35) and an override that fell through to a unit-weight BFS would be an option dropped on the floor. On the relax route `cutoff` travels as `P.cutoffBits`, the f32 bit pattern of `Math.fround(cutoff)` (`+Inf` when absent; a negative cutoff makes `nd > cutoff` skip every relaxation, the source alone, exactly as the CPU port), and the f32 oracle compares against the same `fround`ed value.

- [ ] **Step 4: PD-20 -- the near-far structure, and why the far pile is not compacted**

The queue buffers: `queueOut` of `2 x cap` words with `cap = roundUp(max(1, arcCount), 64)` (never zero: the package binds no zero-length buffer) (the near half at word 0, the far half at word `cap`, so the far half's byte offset is 256-aligned and `dedupe` can bind it alone), and two `n`-word deduped piles `nearIn` and `farIn` (the `Frontier`'s two vertex queues serve as `nearIn` and `farIn`, so the `Frontier` class is reused as is: `vertices[0]` is `nearIn`, `vertices[1]` is `farIn`, and `swap()` is never called). `8 x cap` bytes above `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` is `E_TOO_LARGE { path: "sssp.queue" }` up front (DEP-P8-E: the relax is never windowed). The words of the block in their SSSP reading: `frontierCount` (0) = the deduped near count, `nextFrontierCount` (1) = the near half's appends (unclamped; the detector), `prevDegreeSum` (4) = `prevThresholdBits`, `level` (11) = rounds, `direction` (14) = the round's mode (0 near, 1 far), `done` (15) = 1 finished / 2 a pile overflowed / 3 the threshold absorbed the delta in f32, `farCount` (20) = the deduped far count, `nextFarCount` (21) = the far half's appends, `thresholdBits` (22), `deltaBits` (23).

A round: `frontier-finalize` role 2 looks at the two halves. Near half non-empty: zero word 0, set mode 0, write the dedupe slots (`SLOT 0` claim, `SLOT 1` filter over the near half, count word 1, capacity `cap`, output `nearIn` with count word 0). Near half empty and far half not: `prevThreshold = threshold; threshold += delta` (an f32 add on the bit patterns, in the one lane), zero word 20, set mode 1, write the dedupe slots (`SLOT 3`, `SLOT 4`) over the far half into `farIn` with count word 20. Both empty: `done = 1` and every slot of the round zeroed. A boundary that finds `done` already non-zero zeroes the round's slots and returns without touching a word (P8-T4 Step 6's rule (1); the rounds the host records past the end are no-ops). Either half above `cap`: `done = 2` and the host raises `E_TOO_LARGE { path: "sssp.pile" }`. That refusal is REACHABLE, and the plan says so rather than calling it a detector: the near half is deduped every round, but the far half accumulates one entry per improving relaxation that lands beyond the threshold across every near round of a bucket and is only deduped at the split, so a vertex improved `k` times inside one bucket sits in it `k` times, and nothing bounds `k` by `arcCount` in general. On every fixture of Step 6 the far half stays far below `cap` (an improvement per vertex is rare once the bucket is narrow: `delta` holds about `32 / avgDegree` average hops), and the limit is recorded in G8 as a finding with the largest far-half count the suite observed (read from word 21 through `onRound`); if a real input ever reaches it, the fix is a dedupe of the far half at every near boundary (two more dispatches per round), not a larger `cap`. The seed: `reset(queue, source, { nextFrontierCount: 1, thresholdBits: deltaBits, deltaBits })` -- `level` is NOT seeded to `U32_MAX` (built 2026-09-25): role 2 counts a round only when it picks a mode and never at the done boundary, so a zero seed makes `level` read the number of relax rounds dispatched (67 on the weight-2 path of Step 6), where the BFS seed would read one fewer -- with the source written into the RAW near half (`queueOut[0]`, not `nearIn`) by one `queue.writeBuffer` of the driver's own beyond `reset`'s (`reset` writes `vertices[0][0] = source`, which the SSSP driver ignores: the first dedupe overwrites `nearIn`), so the first role-2 boundary dedupes a one-entry near half into `nearIn` exactly as every later round does and no round is special. Role 2 adds 1 to `level` on every boundary that picks a mode and NOT on the one that sets `done`, so `level` at the end is the number of relax rounds dispatched (BFS's role 0 counts the `done` boundary too, because P8-T6's `levels` formula wants it; the two roles are two rules, each written where its driver reads the word). Then `dedupe-claim`, `dedupe-filter` (P8-T3's, reading their count from the device word `P.countIndex` names). Then role 3 sizes the relax: mode 0 writes `SLOT 2` from word 0 and zeroes word 1 (the near half restarts); mode 1 writes `SLOT 5` from word 20 and zeroes word 21 (the far half restarts, because the pass-through re-appends what stays far). Then `sssp-relax` is dispatched twice, `SLOT 2` in role 0 over `nearIn` and `SLOT 5` in role 1 over `farIn`; one of them runs. Eight dispatches per round as built (both halves' dedupe pairs are recorded every round, the pair of the half not chosen dispatching zero workgroups from its `(0, 0, 1)` slots), four params records per round (role 2, the two dedupe records, role 3) plus the two relax records shared by every round of a submit (`RING_SLOTS = 4 x MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT + 40` in `sssp.ts`, the predecessor batch's 35 records included), `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT` rounds per submit, one four-byte readback of `done` per submit (the whole block only while `onRound` is set, and once more on the error path to name the pile that overflowed). The `Frontier`'s own edge queue (4 x `cap` bytes, leased by `prepareFrontier` because `recordFinalize` takes `P.edgeCapacity` from `frontier.edgeCapacity`) is unused on this route; the raw halves are a separate 8 x `cap` lease. Two guards the BFS loop's shape does not supply on its own: the threshold can stop growing in f32 (`threshold + delta == threshold` once `delta` is below the threshold's ulp, e.g. `delta 8` past `2^27`), and a far pile would then be re-bucketed forever, so role 2 sets `done = 3` when the f32 add returns the threshold unchanged and the host throws `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "sssp.thresholdAbsorbed" }`; and the submit loop carries an explicit bound, derived rather than borrowed: each near round settles at least one vertex of its bucket and there is one pass-through per bucket, so the rounds are at most `n + ceil(sum / delta) + 1` (`sum` the f64 sum of the vector in use bounds every finite distance), and more than `ceil((n + ceil(sum / delta) + 2) / MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) + 1` submits is `E_VALIDATION { label: "sssp/rounds" }` -- an unset `done` past that is a kernel bug, never a hang. `P.stride` is `roundUp(n, WG)`, never `U32_MAX`: the relax grid-strides under an indirect dispatch whose count the host never sees, a deduped pile is at most `n` entries, so a stride of `roundUp(n, WG)` covers the whole dispatch in one trip per lane and `i + stride` never wraps (`U32_MAX` would wrap and loop forever). Why no `compact`: the far pile is re-bucketed by the relax kernel's pass-through role, which reads each entry's settled distance and re-appends it to the near or the far half, and drops an entry whose distance fell below the PREVIOUS threshold (it was appended to near at that improvement and relaxed there). A compaction would need per-entry flags evaluated against a threshold that only exists at split time, so nobody could have written them earlier; one pass over the far pile does the same work in one dispatch instead of a flags pass, a scan and a scatter. The near pile is ONE pile: the sixteen sub-partitions of design 8.4 are a bucket-ordering refinement inside the near band that changes no answer, and they are left for a measurement to ask for.

```ts
export const ssspRelaxWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn sssp_relax(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let first = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    let count = min(atomicLoad(&counters[select(0u, 20u, P.role == 1u)]), P.n);   // the deduped near or far pile
    let threshold = bitcast<f32>(atomicLoad(&counters[22]));
    let cutoff = bitcast<f32>(P.cutoffBits);
    for (var i = first; i < count; i = i + P.stride) {                // no barrier anywhere: the loops may be per lane
        let u = queueIn[i];
        let du = bitcast<f32>(atomicLoad(&dist[u]));
        if (P.role == 1u) {                                            // the pass-through (PD-20): re-bucket a far entry
            if (du < bitcast<f32>(atomicLoad(&counters[4]))) { continue; }   // below the previous threshold: relaxed in an earlier bucket
            if (du < threshold) {
                let q = atomicAdd(&counters[1], 1u);
                if (q < P.edgeCapacity) { queueOut[q] = u; }
            } else {
                let q = atomicAdd(&counters[21], 1u);
                if (q < P.edgeCapacity) { queueOut[P.edgeCapacity + q] = u; }
            }
            continue;
        }
        let end = rowPtr[u + 1u];
        for (var a = rowPtr[u]; a < end; a = a + 1u) {                 // the whole row: never windowed (DEP-P8-E)
            let v = colIdx[a];
            let nd = du + select(1.0, weights[a], HAS_WEIGHTS);         // ONE f32 add (PD-9)
            if (nd > cutoff) { continue; }                             // SsspOptions.cutoff: the CPU port's dv <= cutoff
            let bits = bitcast<u32>(nd);
            let old = atomicMin(&dist[v], bits);                       // exact on non-negative floats
            if (bits < old) {                                          // this lane improved v, so it owns the append
                if (nd < threshold) {
                    let q = atomicAdd(&counters[1], 1u);
                    if (q < P.edgeCapacity) { queueOut[q] = v; }
                } else {
                    let q = atomicAdd(&counters[21], 1u);
                    if (q < P.edgeCapacity) { queueOut[P.edgeCapacity + q] = v; }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
`;
```

Bindings: the four graph slots (`weights` real, `HAS_WEIGHTS` true on every weighted run; the unit-weight case never reaches this kernel), `(1,0) dist rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) queueIn ro array<u32>`, `(1,3) queueOut rw array<u32>`, `(2,0) P FrontierParams` (`edgeCapacity = cap`, `stride`, `n`, `cutoffBits`, `role`); storage count 8. The appends are per improving relaxation, one atomic each: inside a per-lane arc loop no workgroup aggregation is possible without the uniform-strip structure of `bfs-fused`, and Davidson's kernel appends per thread too; the block-mapped form is the optimisation P9 may ask for.

- [ ] **Step 5: PD-11 and PD-27 -- the predecessor pass, and why its chain always reaches the source**

The 2026-09-23 rule, "the smallest arc index that attains `dist[v]`", can write a CYCLE. Undirected `0-1` at weight 0 and `1-2` at weight 1 from source 2: `dist = [1, 1, 0]`; arc 0 (`0 -> 1`) attains node 1 and is smaller than arc 3 (`2 -> 1`), arc 1 (`1 -> 0`) attains node 0, so `pred[1] = arc 0`, `pred[0] = arc 1`, and `walkPredArcs` (`algorithms/src/indexed/dijkstra.ts` lines 57-60, an unbounded `while (node !== source)`) alternates 1, 0, 1 forever -- the exact path graphty-element's `DijkstraAlgorithm` takes (`pathTo`, line 158). Strictly positive weights do it too: at `dist[u] = 2^24` a unit arc gives `Math.fround(2 ** 24 + 1) === 2 ** 24`, so a unit-weight path past that distance is one plateau of equal distances and the same tie rule loops inside it. A tight arc never lowers the distance (a non-negative `w` and a monotone `fround` cannot), so every such cycle lives inside a PLATEAU -- the reached nodes at one distance, joined by tight arcs -- and a rule that looks only at arcs cannot see it.

The rule that replaces it (PD-27). A TIGHT arc is `(u, v, w)` with `u` and `v` reached, `v` not the source, and `bitcast<u32>(bitcast<f32>(dist[u]) + w) == dist[v]` (one f32 add, compared as bits, PD-9). Every reached node gets a KEY, and `predArc[v]` is the smallest tight arc whose source sits exactly one key step below `v`:

- `sssp` (`P.mode 0`, the plateau rule): the key is `(dist[v], hops[v])`, compared lexicographically. A ROOT is the source or a node with a tight in-arc from a STRICTLY SMALLER distance; its `hops` is 0. Every other reached node's `hops` is the fewest tight EQUAL-distance arcs from a root of its plateau. The admitted arcs: into a root, the tight arcs from a strictly smaller distance; into a non-root, the tight equal-distance arcs from a node whose `hops` is one less.
- `bellmanFord` (`P.mode 1`, the tight-subgraph rule): a tight arc with a negative weight steps to a LARGER distance, so `dist` is no key there; the key is `hops[v]` alone, the fewest tight arcs from the source over ALL tight arcs, and the admitted arcs are the tight arcs from a node whose `hops` is one less.

Why the chain ends at the source, in words a builder can check. (1) Along `predArc` the key strictly decreases: a plateau-rule step goes to a strictly smaller `dist` (a root's arc) or to the same `dist` and `hops - 1`; a tight-subgraph step goes to `hops - 1`. A finite walk on which a key strictly decreases cannot revisit a node, so there is no cycle, and the walk stops only at a node with no `predArc`. (2) The only reached node with no `predArc` is the source. Under the plateau rule: take any plateau at a distance `D > 0` and the FIRST of its nodes, `v`, to be written with `D`, by a relaxation `(u, v, w)`. At that moment `dist[u] <= D` (the add cannot lower a value). The arc is still tight once everything has settled: `dist[u]` can only fall, which cannot raise `fround(dist[u] + w)` above `D`, and the fixed point (every arc offered with the final `dist[u]`, which the near-far loop guarantees) keeps it at least `D`. And `dist[u]` settles STRICTLY below `D`: had it settled at `D`, `u` held `D` when it wrote `v`, so `u` was written with `D` before `v`, which contradicts "first". So `v` is a root. Every other node of the plateau was written with `D` by a node holding at most `D` -- a strictly smaller one makes it a root, an equal one is a plateau node written earlier, over an arc that is tight and equal-distance at the end by the same argument -- so by induction on the order the nodes were written each has a chain of tight equal-distance arcs back to a root, and its `hops` is finite. The plateau at `D = 0` has the source as its root by seeding. So every reached non-source node has an admitted in-arc, and `predArc` names the smallest. Under the tight-subgraph rule the same induction runs over all reached nodes without the plateau restriction and gives every one of them finite `hops` -- EXCEPT when f32 rounding let a cycle improve a distance once (a directed `s -> a` at `2^24`, `a -> b` at `+1`, `b -> a` at `-1`: `dist[a]` ends at `2^24 - 1` and `s -> a` is no longer tight), which exact arithmetic never does and which P8-T10 Step 3 detects and refuses. (3) The chain is asserted, never assumed: `expectPredChainReachesSource` (P8-T2 Step 3) walks every reached node to the source with a step bound of `n` and fails, rather than hangs, when it does not arrive.

The pass. `sssp-pred` in `MODE 0` (P8-T6 Step 3's body) runs three roles over a `pred` buffer of `2 x hb + 64` words, `hb = roundUp(n, 64)`: `pred[0, n)` the arcs, `pred[hb, hb + n)` the hop counts, `pred[2 hb]` the changed word, `pred[2 hb + 1]` the orphan word. Setup, once the piles are empty: `fill` the whole buffer with `INVALID_INDEX`, submit, then `queue.writeBuffer` of word `hb + source` to 0 (the source is the one seeded root) and of the two flag words to 0. Then ONE batch: role 0 (the roots pass; `P.mode 0` only) once; `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT` dispatches of role 1 with `P.iteration = 0 .. 31` -- a hop pass lowers `hops[v]` to `hops[u] + 1` along every admitted arc and records its index in the changed word when it lowered one, and a pass whose predecessor pass changed nothing returns at its first line, so a run with no plateau pays ONE full pass and 31 empty ones; `fill` of `pred[0, n)` with `INVALID_INDEX`; role 2 (the predecessor pass); the readbacks of `dist`, `pred[0, n)`, the two flag words and the counters. Every dispatch is `planGridStride(n)` with `P.stride` from the plan, `P.source`, `P.predKind 0`, the run's weights bound and `HAS_WEIGHTS` set (Step 3). ONE `mapAsync`, the result batch's. When the changed word reads `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT` the last hop pass still changed something: zero the two words by `queue.writeBuffer` and record the batch again without role 0 (`hops` persists); on every fixture of Step 6 the first batch suffices, and a plateau `k` hops deep needs `ceil((k + 1) / 32)` batches. The re-record is bounded as the BFS submit loop is (P8-T6 Step 4): more than `ceil((n + 1) / MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) + 1` batches is `E_VALIDATION { label: "sssp/pred" }`, because a hop count is at most `n` and a changed word that keeps reading 32 past that is a kernel bug, never a result. When the orphan word is not zero the driver throws `E_VALIDATION { label: "sssp/pred" }`: (2) says it cannot happen with non-negative weights, so it is a kernel bug, never a result. The batch is 35 records, inside `RING_SLOTS`. `predArc` is `pred[0, n)`; `reachedCount` is the count of finite `dist`. P8-T12 dispatches every role once per window with only `arcBase` / `arcEnd` changing.

The result is bitwise reproducible (PD-14): the key is a function of the settled `dist` alone, and `atomicMin` makes the smallest admitted arc the winner in whatever order the lanes arrive. Packing the arc index into the distance atomic is impossible (WGSL has no 64-bit atomic, and a two-word "atomic" is not atomic). Recording the relaxing arc at claim time was rejected for two reasons: the arc that produces the final value FIRST is a scheduling accident whenever two arcs produce the same f32 sum, so it is not reproducible; and the pred write cannot be atomic with the dist write, so a stale pred can point back into the very plateau cycle above. What the seam walks is therefore a tree by construction, which is what its `SsspResultLike` promises; the CPU port's `predArc` is the arc of the LAST relaxation under heap order, a different valid tree, so the test asserts `expectPredArcAttains` (the rule recomputed on the host) and never equality with the CPU.

- [ ] **Step 2: PD-12 -- the structs, the body, and why the compare-exchange must be bounded**

`BF_PARAMS` (`BfParams`, uniform, 16 B): `edgeCount` @0, `stride` @4, `maxRetries` @8, `cutoffBits` @12. `BF_FLAGS` (`BfFlags`, storage, 16 B): `changed` @0, `retryExhausted` @4, `pad0` @8, `pad1` @12. `atomicMin` on bit patterns works only for non-negative floats; with a negative distance the bit-pattern order reverses. So the relax is a compare-exchange loop on the bit pattern: read, compute, compare as floats, try to exchange. WGSL 17.8.5 allows `atomicCompareExchangeWeak` to fail spuriously, so an unbounded loop can in principle spin; the bound is `MAX_RETRIES = 16` (a module-level `const`; contention is per vertex, not global), and a lane that exhausts it sets `retryExhausted`. The driver then treats the batch as changed and runs on, so a lost update is retried by the next round that examines every edge anyway; a bound hit in the decision round is `E_VALIDATION`, never a guess. This is the discipline the connected-components link loop uses (`MAX_STEPS` in `components.ts`).

```ts
export const bfRelaxWgsl = /* wgsl */ `
fn relax(v: u32, nd: f32) {
    var cur = atomicLoad(&dist[v]);
    var tries = 0u;
    loop {
        if (!(nd < bitcast<f32>(cur))) { break; }                     // no improvement; +Inf is greater than every finite nd
        let r = atomicCompareExchangeWeak(&dist[v], cur, bitcast<u32>(nd));
        if (r.exchanged) { atomicStore(&flags[0], 1u); break; }        // changed
        cur = r.old_value;
        tries = tries + 1u;
        if (tries >= P.maxRetries) { atomicStore(&flags[1], 1u); break; }   // retryExhausted (PD-12)
    }
}

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn bf_relax(@builtin(workgroup_id) wid: vec3<u32>, @builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    let first = linear_id(wid, lid.x);
    let cutoff = bitcast<f32>(P.cutoffBits);
    for (var e = first; e < P.edgeCount; e = e + P.stride) {          // each logical edge once (edgeList)
        let u = edgeSrc[e];
        let v = edgeDst[e];
        let w = weights[edgeToArc[e]];                                 // the edge's weight through its forward arc
        let du = atomicLoad(&dist[u]);
        if (du != F32_INF_BITS) {                                      // unreached is tested as the bit pattern, as sssp-pred does: bitcast<f32>(F32_INF_BITS) is a const-expression Tint rejects as "value inf cannot be represented as 'f32'"
            let nd = bitcast<f32>(du) + w;
            if (nd <= cutoff) { relax(v, nd); }
        }
        if (UNDIRECTED) {                                              // the other direction of an undirected edge
            let dv = atomicLoad(&dist[v]);
            if (dv != F32_INF_BITS) {
                let nd = bitcast<f32>(dv) + w;
                if (nd <= cutoff) { relax(u, nd); }
            }
        }
    }
}
`;
```

Bindings: `(1,0) edgeSrc ro`, `(1,1) edgeDst ro`, `(1,2) edgeToArc ro`, `(1,3) weights ro` (the run's vector: the core's column or the uploaded override, both arc-indexed), `(1,4) dist rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,5) flags rw array<atomic<u32>>` (the `BfFlags` block), `(2,0) P BfParams`; `overrideDecls: [{ name: "UNDIRECTED", type: "bool", default: false }]`; storage count 6; no graph group. `edgeToArc` is NOT always there to bind: graph-format omits it whenever `flags.arcToEdgeIsIdentity` is true (`graph-format/src/types/snapshot.ts` line 170 -- directed AND built from a sorted edge list, `graph-format/src/builder/counting-sort.ts` line 447), residency uploads it only when the segment exists (`src/memory/residency.ts` lines 241-242), and `core()` then hands back `edgeToArc: null` (`CoreBinding` line 59; the SNAPSHOT's own `edgeToArc` getter is never null -- graph-format materialises the identity on first access -- so the null a test asserts is the residency's `core(s, [..., "edgeToArc"]).edgeToArc`, the binding the driver sees) -- which the directed `pathEdges` fixture of Step 4 is (`test/helpers/graphs.ts` line 126: its edge list is in index order, so a directed freeze has an identity `arcToEdge`). Decided: in that case edge `e` IS arc `e`, so the driver binds in slot `(1,2)` an `edgeCount`-word scratch filled once at setup with the identity by the `fill` kernel's mode 1 (`dst[i] = i + P.value` with `value 0`), and the kernel reads `weights[edgeToArc[e]]` unchanged. Nothing in the registry entry, the `TABLE` row, the `STORAGE_COUNTS` row or the pinned compile count 3 moves; an `IDENTITY` override would have moved all four. `finiteWeights === false` is `E_UNSUPPORTED` here too; a vector with no negative entry is legal and gives `sssp`'s answer (Step 4 cross-checks it); a RUN with no weight vector -- no weight column AND no `options.weights` override -- takes the BFS route of P8-T9 (there is nothing negative to relax); an override on an unweighted snapshot is the run's vector and is scanned and relaxed exactly like a column (PD-22: the routing is by the run, never by the snapshot's flags alone). One consequence of the kernel's shape, and the rule it forces: on an UNDIRECTED snapshot the kernel reads ONE weight per logical edge (`weights[edgeToArc[e]]`, the forward arc) and applies it in both directions, because an undirected edge has one weight by definition. A caller-supplied `weights` override whose two arcs of one edge differ therefore means the caller wanted a directed graph, and the driver REFUSES it rather than half-honouring it: the host scan that already walks the override for `allOne` / `nonNegative` / `finite` / `sum` also checks, on an undirected snapshot, `weights[a] === weights[edgeToArc[arcToEdge[a]]]` for every arc `a` (the reverse arc against the forward arc of its edge; the forward arc compares with itself) and throws `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "bellmanFord.asymmetricUndirectedWeights", hint: "use a directed snapshot" }` otherwise, before any device work. The snapshot's own column never trips it (the freeze writes one weight per edge on both arcs). Binding the mirror arc's weight for the reverse relaxation instead was rejected: it would silently change the algorithm's meaning on that input, and `sssp` on the same snapshot honours the same vector per arc, so the two members would disagree on one input with no error (PD-25's rule: implemented or refused, never dropped).

- [ ] **Step 3: The rounds.** `dist` filled with `F32_INF_BITS`, `dist[source] = 0`. Batches of `ROUNDS_PER_BATCH = 8` rounds over `planGridStride(edgeCount)` -- the LAST batch clamped to `min(8, n - 1 - rounds)` rounds, so the reported round count never exceeds `n - 1` (on the directed `pathEdges(64)` of Step 5 it reads exactly 63, not 64 or 65) -- `flags` zeroed by `queue.writeBuffer` before each batch, both words read back after it (one `mapAsync` per batch); the loop stops when `changed === 0` (no negative cycle can reach the source: a reachable negative cycle changes something every round) or when `rounds === n - 1`, in which case ONE more round runs and `changed` after it is `hasNegativeCycle`. `retryExhausted` in any batch counts as changed and is tallied for the test; set in the decision round it is `E_VALIDATION { label: "bellmanFord/retry" }`. Then the predecessor pass of P8-T9 Step 5 under the tight-subgraph rule (`P.mode 1`, PD-27): the same kernel and the same `2 x hb + 64`-word `pred` buffer, `hops[source] = 0` the only seed and NO roots pass (a tight arc with a negative weight steps to a larger distance, so `dist` is no key here); the batched hop passes then run over every tight arc, which costs at most what one batch of the rounds above costs per 32 passes and converges within the tight subgraph's hop depth, and the batch ends with the write role and the readback of `dist`, `pred[0, n)` and the two flag words (the `BfFlags` block was read after the decision round and nothing writes it afterwards, so it is not read again; built 2026-09-25). An ORPHAN -- a reached node the tight subgraph does not reach, counted in the second flag word -- can arise only when f32 rounding let a cycle improve a distance once (the `roundedCycle` fixture of Step 4: `dist[a]` ends at `2^24 - 1` and the source's arc is no longer tight; exact arithmetic has no such run), and the driver refuses it with `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "bellmanFord.roundedCycle", hint: "a cycle of weights below one f32 ulp relaxed once at a distance above 2^24; scale the weights or shorten the distances" }` rather than return a chain that ends short of the source (the seam's walker would then hand `arcSourceIn` an `INVALID_INDEX` and walk garbage). With `hasNegativeCycle` the result carries the last round's `dist` and `predArc` and says so: the pass still runs, its chains are still acyclic (the key argument does not need the distances to be shortest), a node the tight subgraph does not reach keeps `INVALID_INDEX`, and the orphan refusal is skipped, because the result already says its arrays are not shortest paths.

Two override cases: `weights` with non-unit values (integer weights 1..10, seeded) on an UNWEIGHTED snapshot (`pathEdges(500)` with no weight column) gives `dist` bitwise equal to the f32 oracle run with that vector and NOT equal to the BFS depths as f32 (the option is not dropped, PD-22); and on an UNDIRECTED snapshot an override whose two arcs of one edge differ (the symmetric vector with `weights[edgeToArc[arcToEdge[a]]] + 1` written into ONE reverse arc) is `E_UNSUPPORTED` with `details.feature === "bellmanFord.asymmetricUndirectedWeights"`, thrown before any device work (`counter.mapAsyncCalls` unchanged after `verifyDevice` and a reset), while the same override made symmetric again runs and equals `sssp`'s answer bitwise. Three fixtures by name: the directed identity path -- `pathEdges(64)` frozen directed, whose edge list is in index order so `s.flags.arcToEdgeIsIdentity` is true and the residency's `core(s, [..., "edgeToArc"]).edgeToArc === null` (`test/helpers/graphs.ts` line 126; assert both on the fixture, so the case is known to exercise the iota binding of Step 2; the snapshot's own `edgeToArc` getter materialises the identity and is never null, Step 2), with weights `a + 1` per arc so no two arcs share a weight and a wrong index is a `dist` miss, `dist` bitwise equal to the f32 oracle; the two PD-27 counterexamples of P8-T9 Step 6, `zeroPlateau(k)` and `absorbPath(k)`, on which `expectPredChainReachesSource` must pass (the 2026-09-23 rule loops on both); and `roundedCycle` -- directed, `s -> a` at `2^24`, `a -> b` at `+1`, `b -> a` at `-1`, frozen with `ROUNDED_CYCLE_NODES = 8` nodes (built 2026-09-25: the cycle's one improvement is the THIRD relaxation of the walk `s -> a -> b -> a`, so with exactly three nodes the device's `n - 1 = 2` synchronous rounds leave it to the decision round, which then reports a negative cycle -- an equally honest f32 answer, but a schedule-dependent one, since a lane that runs after its source's lane settles it a round earlier; five spare nodes give the rounds room to settle whatever the schedule) -- on which the f32 oracle settles at `[0, 2^24 - 1, 2^24]` with the flag false and the call throws `E_UNSUPPORTED` with `details.feature === "bellmanFord.roundedCycle"`, written before the orphan word exists so the builder watches the chain check fail on `a` first. `dist` bitwise against the f32 oracle, within the derived tolerance of the f64 one, and cross-checked bitwise against `sssp` on the same graph when all weights are non-negative; `expectPredArcAttains` with rule `"tight"` and `expectPredChainReachesSource` on every fixture whose flag is false; run-twice bitwise on `dist`, `pred` and the flags where the flag is false, and where it is TRUE the flag, the reached set and `reachedCount` twice plus acyclic chains (built 2026-09-25: with a reachable negative cycle `dist` is the last round's, and which improvement of the cycle landed last is the schedule's, so the arrays are not held bitwise there); `retryExhaustedRounds === 0` on every fixture, so the bound is known to be adequate rather than assumed. Every readback names its buffer.

### Task P8-T11: Closeness, harmonic closeness and eccentricity

**Depends on P8-T6 (and P8-T3's `compact`); benefits from P8-T8 but does not require it. What ships: closeness ALONE (DEP-P8-F, PD-19); the title is the 2026-09-23 plan's and the harmonic and eccentricity reductions are dropped, not deferred.**

**Spec:** design 8.4 (32 sources per `u32` word as a bit-parallel frontier for unweighted graphs, repeated near-far for weighted, per-source rows reduced on the device without materialising `n x n`, batch size from `maxBufferSize`); design 3.3 line 810; design 9.7 (`1e-5`, integer distances before division); the seam's `closenessCentrality(s, options?: HitsOptionsLike)`.

**Files:** Create `$PKG/src/wgsl/closeness-sweep.wgsl.ts`, `$PKG/src/wgsl/closeness-reduce.wgsl.ts`, `$PKG/src/algorithms/closeness.ts`, `$PKG/test/helpers/closeness.ts` (`runSweep` through the inspect seam, the funnel fixture, the hand-seeded reduce run, `closenessReport`), `$PKG/test/algorithms/closeness.test.ts`, `$PKG/test/sabotage/closeness.test.ts`; modify `$PKG/src/kernels.ts` (two entries), `$PKG/test/kernel/bind-group-budget.test.ts` (`"closeness-sweep": 8, "closeness-reduce": 3`), `$PKG/test/kernel/registry.test.ts`, `$PKG/test/kernel/wgsl-compile.test.ts` (pins 5 and 1), `$PKG/test/helpers/override-matrix.ts`, `$PKG/test/helpers/sabotage.ts`. Consumes `prepareCompact.record`, P4's `indirect-finalize` (`INDIRECT_PARAMS`: `countIndex`, `wg`, `slot`), `fill`, `sssp` (the weighted case), the legacy `closenessCentrality` of `@graphty/algorithms` (built in Step 0) as the parity target on karate, `closenessOracle`.

**Interfaces produced:** `closenessCentrality(ctx, s, options?: HitsOptionsLike & GpuRunOptions): Promise<GpuScoresResult>`; the `@internal` tuning entry `closenessWithTuning(ctx, s, options, tuning: { levelsPerSubmit?, onBatch?(batchStart, perSource: U32) })` (PD-26's shape), which is how Step 5 reads `perSource` back, plus the `@internal` constants `SOURCES_PER_BATCH` (32) and `PER_SOURCE_WORDS` (128).

Expected: FAIL with `Error: Cannot find module '../../src/algorithms/closeness.js' imported from .../test/algorithms/closeness.test.ts` (the headline; `Failed to load url ../../src/algorithms/closeness.js` is the cause line, as P8-T3 recorded).

- [ ] **Step 2: PD-13 and PD-25 -- one driver, one output, the seam's options.** `score[s] = 1 / sumDist_s`, with `sumDist_s` the exact integer (or, weighted, f32) sum of the finite distances from `s` to every OTHER node -- an unreached node adds nothing -- and `0` when nothing is reached (`sumDist_s === 0`). NO reached factor and NO Wasserman-Faust scaling. The host reduction is one line per source over the `perSource` readback: `scores[s] = sum === 0 ? 0 : 1 / sum`, computed in f64 from the exact 64-bit integer sum (`sumHi x 2^32 + sumLo`) and stored as `F32` with `precision: "f32"`; `reached` is read back and compared exactly but does not enter the score. This is EXACTLY the legacy default (`normalized: false`) of `closenessCentrality` in `@graphty/algorithms` (`algorithms/src/algorithms/centrality/closeness.ts` lines 74-86, 114), which graphty-element calls with no options and publishes as `normalization: "none"` (`graphty-element/src/algorithms/ClosenessCentralityAlgorithm.ts` lines 53, 66), and which design 9.7 (line 3326) fixes as the target with "as today" -- so the number the element's closeness panel shows does not change, and a future `indexed.closenessCentrality` port has one number to match. The NetworkX / Wasserman-Faust form, `reached_s / sumDist_s x reached_s / (n - 1)`, is explicitly NOT offered: on karate (connected, 34 nodes, `reached_s = 33` everywhere) it is 33x the legacy number for every node, so it could never have been substituted silently -- the Step 5 parity assertion against the legacy function would fail on every score -- and changing what a panel shows is a product decision, not this phase's; P8-T16's seam record says so. `weighted` defaults to `s.flags.weighted`; when true AND the snapshot's weights are not all one, the run is one `sssp` per source with the per-source sums reduced on the host (design 8.4's own answer, slow and correct; `nonNegativeWeights === false` is `E_UNSUPPORTED`); `weighted: true` over `flags.allWeightsOne` (or no weight column) takes the bit-parallel sweep, one host branch, because every one of those `sssp` calls would route to a BFS anyway and the sweep gives the identical answer at roughly `1 / n` the cost; `weighted: false` on a WEIGHTED snapshot ignores the column by request and takes the unweighted bit-parallel sweep -- that is honouring the option, and the snapshot's flag alone must not route it through `sssp`. `maxIterations` and `tolerance` are the seam's placeholder keys and an exact traversal has neither, so the driver REFUSES them before any device work: `options.maxIterations !== undefined` throws `E_UNSUPPORTED { option: "maxIterations", hint: "closeness is an exact traversal; the option has no meaning here" }`, and `tolerance` likewise with `{ option: "tolerance" }` -- the `{ option }` form `src/errors.ts` spells and `resolveForceAtlas2Options` uses for `nodeSize` (`src/layouts/forceatlas2.ts` lines 155-167). `undefined` is legal for both, so the element's no-option call and a bare `HitsOptionsLike` bag pass. That is a recorded choice (PD-25; P8-T16's seam record): a caller's option is either implemented or refused, never dropped on the floor, and nothing forwards a bag into this member unseen -- `accelerated(acc)` has no `closenessCentrality` method (section 0.1), and the port that one day adds one brings its real option type, at which point these keys leave the type. `iterations` reports the source batches run, `converged` is always `true`. `dest` is `scores`.

- [ ] **Step 3: The bit-parallel sweep.** Sources are taken 32 per batch (`ceil(n / 32)` batches). The batch's state is one `bits` buffer of four regions of `bitsBase = roundUp(n, 64)` words each -- `visited` at 0, `frontier` at `bitsBase`, `next` at `2 x bitsBase`, `flags` at `3 x bitsBase` (each region's byte offset is 256-aligned, so `fill` and `compact` can bind one alone) -- with bit `s` of word `v` meaning "source `s` has reached / is at / is next at `v`"; a `frontierList` of `n` words with its count in `counters[0]`; and `perSource`, 128 words: `newCount[32]` @0, `reached[32]` @32, `sumLo[32]` @64, `sumHi[32]` @96. The sweep is `advance-expand`'s block-mapped expansion (P8-T5 Step 2: the strip copied textually; the scan INLINED as `bfs-contract`'s Hillis-Steele, not the `wg_scan_u32` call, because this kernel is not a twin kernel and declares `needs: []`, P8-T1 Step 5) with the claim inline: for the arc `(u, x)`, `mask = frontier[u] & ~visited[x]`; if non-zero, `old = atomicOr(&visited[x], mask)`, `fresh = mask & ~old`; if non-zero, `atomicOr(&bits[nextBase + x], fresh)` with `let nextBase = select(2u * P.bitsBase, P.bitsBase, P.mode == 1u);` (the region that is `next` this level), `atomicStore(&bits[3u * P.bitsBase + x], 1u)` (the flags region is part of the one `array<atomic<u32>>` binding, so a plain store is a compile error), and one `atomicAdd` on a WORKGROUP-memory `local[s]` per set bit of `fresh` (`firstTrailingBit` and `b & (b - 1u)`), flushed after the strip loop by lanes 0..31 into `perSource.newCount` -- one global atomic per source per workgroup, never one per arc. The loop's bound is the `workgroupUniformLoad` aggregate, so the flush's barrier is uniform. Bindings: the four graph slots, `(1,0) frontierList ro array<u32>`, `(1,1) counters rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,2) bits rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(1,3) perSource rw array<atomic<u32>>`, `(2,0) P` (`bitsBase`, `arcBase 0`, `arcEnd arcCount`; never windowed); storage count 8. The frontier and next regions swap by parity through `P.mode` (`frontierBase = 3u * P.bitsBase - nextBase`), so no copy runs between levels; the sabotage row `next-bits-not-set` therefore pins the `select`ed form, never a literal region.

`closeness-reduce`, one lane, two roles by `P.role`: role 0 (per level, after the sweep) sets `done = (counters[0] == 0)`, then for each `s`: `reached[s] += newCount[s]`, `sum[s] += newCount[s] x (level + 1)` in 64 bits (`sumLo` / `sumHi` with the 16-bit-split product, because `count x level` exceeds 2^32 on a long path and an exact integer is what design 9.7 asks for before the division), `newCount[s] = 0`, and `level += 1`; role 1 (once per batch, the seed) writes for `s` in `0 .. min(32, n - batchStart)`: bit `s` into `visited[source_s]` and `frontier[source_s]`, `flags[source_s] = 1`, `counters[0] = k`, and `level = U32_MAX`. The seed writes the FLAGS, not the frontier list, and seeds `level` at `U32_MAX`, for the same reason P8-T4 Step 4 seeds `nextFrontierCount` rather than `frontierCount`: the level runs `closeness-reduce` role 0 and then `compact` BEFORE the sweep, so a seeded `frontierList` would be overwritten by a compaction of all-zero flags (`counters[0]` becomes 0 and the first sweep dispatches nothing), and a seeded `level 0` would make role 0 at level 1 accumulate the distance-1 nodes as `newCount x (level + 1) == 2`. With the seed as written, level 0's role 0 sees `counters[0] == k` (not done), accumulates nothing (`newCount` is 0) and brings `level` to 0; `compact` turns the seeded flags into `frontierList = sources`; the sweep claims at distance 1; and level 1's role 0 accumulates `newCount x 1`. The `distance-is-the-level` sabotage row pins `let d = level + 1u;` and needs no change. Bindings `(1,0) counters rw`, `(1,1) perSource rw`, `(1,2) bits rw` (plain `array<u32>`: one lane writes the seed), `(2,0) P`; storage count 3 (the seed role writes `bits`, which is why it is not 2; it never touches the frontier list, so the `frontierList` slot the 2026-09-23 table gave this kernel is gone). The seed's `batchStart` travels in `P.source` and the level's parity in `P.mode`; `counters` is a 96-byte scratch in the `FrontierCounters` layout (word 0 the list's count, word 11 `level`, word 15 `done`, decoded with `FRONTIER_COUNTERS.read` and indexed through `W`), not a `Frontier`.

A level, all host-recorded, `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT` per submit with one four-byte readback of `done`, in this order because the flags must be compacted before they are cleared: `closeness-reduce` role 0 (`done = (counters[0] == 0)` reads the PREVIOUS level's compacted count, so the traversal ends one level after the last claim, which is one empty sweep and no wrong sum); `compact.record(pass, { queue: iota, flags: bits[flags region], count: n, out: frontierList, outCount: counters, outIndex: 0 })`; `indirect-finalize` (`countIndex 0`, the sweep's slot); `fill` the OLD frontier region (it becomes next) and the `flags` region to 0; `closeness-sweep` (indirect). Three details the bodies must spell, since they are given in prose: the workgroup-memory `local[32]` is zeroed before the strip loop (WebGPU zero-initialises workgroup memory, but say it); the 64-bit `count x d` is spelled as the 16-bit split into `lo` / `hi` before the carry; and at level 0 the region the `fill` clears is the NEXT region of that level (`2 x bitsBase`, since the seed put the sources at `bitsBase`), never the seeded one. A batch: `fill` all four regions and `perSource` to 0, `closeness-reduce` role 1 (the seed), the levels until `done`, one readback of `perSource` (512 B) folded into the host's score loop (as built, the block rides in EVERY submit's readback beside the `done` word -- both are requests of one batch, one map -- so the finished batch needs no extra map: `ceil(levels / 32)` maps per batch and none for the setup). Two details the builder met: `compact.record` leases its offsets and the scan's block sums afresh on every call, once per LEVEL here, and a lease lives until the scope's dispose, so the planner is given a scope whose `scratch` hands the same buffer back for the same label and size (the dispatches of one pass run in order, so a level's scan may overwrite the previous level's); and the boundary, finalize, fill and sweep records are written once per SUBMIT and shared by its levels (`RING_SLOTS = 8 x MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT + 16`, the 8 being compact's per-level records). Cost, stated so nobody is surprised: closeness is `O(n x m)` on any device; at 1M nodes it is 31,250 batches of a full multi-source traversal, minutes on the card, and no target in design 10.4 asks for less.

- [ ] **Step 4: The weighted case** is repeated `sssp`: one call per source, the sums reduced on the host between calls. Slow and correct; it is the design's own answer and this phase does not improve on it.

- [ ] **Step 5: Proof.** Against `closenessOracle` (reading `perSource` back by its binding -- through the driver's `onBatch` inspect seam, which hands the test every batch's 128-word block, folded by `test/helpers/closeness.ts`'s `runSweep` into per-node `reached`, `sum` and `newCount` arrays -- and comparing the INTEGER sums and reached counts per source exactly, then the f64 scores within `1e-5` relative): `pathEdges(n)` and `starEdges(k)` have closed-form answers (`2 / (n(n-1))` at a path end, `1 / k` at the hub and `1 / (2k - 1)` at a leaf, P8-T2 Step 4) and both are asserted analytically, not just differentially; `KARATE_EDGES` against the oracle AND against the legacy `closenessCentrality` from `@graphty/algorithms` called with NO options on the same graph as an object graph, score for score within `1e-5` relative (the parity target of Step 2: the legacy `1 / sum`, so a NetworkX-form driver fails here by a factor of 33 on every node); a disconnected graph, where the "unreached excluded" rule (an unreached node adds nothing to the sum, and an isolated node scores `0`, never `1 / 0`) is the only thing separating a right answer from a plausible one, also run through the legacy function; a graph of 70 nodes (three batches, one partial); `weighted: true` on the weighted karate against the f64 oracle within the derived SSSP tolerance; the weighted karate with `weighted: false` equal bitwise on `perSource` and `scores` to the unweighted karate with no options (the column ignored by request); `maxIterations: 1` is `E_UNSUPPORTED` with `details.option === "maxIterations"` and `tolerance: 1e-3` is `E_UNSUPPORTED` with `details.option === "tolerance"`, both thrown before any device work (`counter.mapAsyncCalls` unchanged after `verifyDevice` and a reset), while `{ maxIterations: undefined, tolerance: undefined }` runs and equals the no-option result. Run-twice bitwise on `perSource` -- an exact quantity -- and on `scores`.

One case proves the 64-bit accumulation of `closeness-reduce` role 0 with no graph at all, because no fixture a test can run overflows it: the per-source sum from an end of `pathEdges(n)` is `n(n-1)/2`, below 2^32 until `n > 92,682`, and closeness on a path that long is `ceil(n / 32)` batches of about `n / 2` levels of eight dispatches each -- hundreds of millions of dispatches, hours, not a test. So the case binds `closeness-reduce` by hand the way P8-T6 Step 5 hand-binds `sssp-pred` (`ctx.pipelines.kernel(kernelSpec("closeness-reduce"))`, `P.role = 0`), with the counters block uploaded as a `Uint32Array` whose `counters[0]` is `1` (not done) and whose `level` word is `0xFFFF` (so `d = level + 1 = 65536 = 2^16`), `perSource` uploaded as a `Uint32Array` of 128 words that are all zero except `newCount[0] = 0x10001` (65537), `sumLo[0] = 0xFFFFF000` and `sumHi[0] = 0`, and `bits` and `frontierList` bound to any scratch buffers (role 0 never reads them). Dispatch ONE workgroup (`plan1d(WG, WG, caps)`), read `perSource` and the counters back by name and assert with BigInt: the product `65537 x 65536 = 0x1_0001_0000` is above 2^32 (high word `1`, low word `0x10000`), the low add `0xFFFFF000 + 0x10000` wraps to `0xF000` with a carry, so `sumHi[0] === 2`, `sumLo[0] === 0xF000`, and `(BigInt(sumHi[0]) << 32n) | BigInt(sumLo[0])` equals `0xFFFFF000n + 65537n * 65536n`, which is `8589996032n`; `reached[0] === 65537`, `newCount[0] === 0`, the `level` word `0x10000`, `done === 0`, and every other source's four words still `0`. With the carry dropped the high word reads `1` and the sum `4295028736n`; with the product's high word dropped it reads `1` too; either is a miss on the BigInt equality. Write it before the body exists and watch it fail on the missing kernel first.

- [ ] **Step 6: Three sabotage rows per kernel**

```ts
    "closeness-sweep": Object.freeze([
        { name: "already-visited-recounted", find: "let fresh = mask & ~old;", replace: "let fresh = mask;", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
        { name: "next-bits-not-set", find: "atomicOr(&bits[nextBase + x], fresh);", replace: "atomicOr(&bits[nextBase + x], 0u);", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
        { name: "source-word-not-bit", find: "let s = firstTrailingBit(b);", replace: "let s = 0u;", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
    ]),
    "closeness-reduce": Object.freeze([
        { name: "distance-is-the-level", find: "let d = level + 1u;", replace: "let d = level;", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
        { name: "reached-not-accumulated", find: "atomicLoad(&perSource[32u + s]) + c", replace: "atomicLoad(&perSource[32u + s])", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
        { name: "carry-dropped", find: "select(0u, 1u, lo < before)", replace: "0u", minFactor: 10, test: CLOSENESS_TEST },
    ]),
```

The `find` strings name the lines the bodies must contain (the 64-bit add is `let before = lo; lo = lo + p; hi = hi + select(0u, 1u, lo < before);`); the `carry-dropped` row is caught by Step 5's hand-bound role-0 dispatch (seeded `newCount[0] = 0x10001`, `sumLo[0] = 0xFFFFF000`, `level = 0xFFFF`; the BigInt sum reads `4295028736n` instead of `8589996032n`), not by a graph: no runnable fixture's per-source sum crosses 2^32, so the `pathEdges(70000)` case of the earlier draft (sum `2.45e9`, no carry ever) could not catch it and is dropped. The report the sabotage suite measures (`closenessReport`) is karate, the 70-node path, the 200-funnel (`0 -- i`, `i -- k + 1`: from source 0 the whole middle layer reaches `k + 1` at once, so the lanes of one SIMD group load its visited word together and a claim counted without its `atomicOr` result is caught deterministically, not by a scheduling accident) and the hand-seeded reduce. Measured 2026-09-25: every row Infinity on lavapipe and on the RTX 4070 SUPER (`already-visited-recounted` already at `karate.reached`, the funnel kept as the deterministic witness; `carry-dropped` at `reduce.sum64`). Expected: FAIL with `WebGpuGraphError: advance: windowed cores are not executed by the pull`, code `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "advance.windowed" }`, from `assertNotWindowed(core, "advance")` inside `prepareAdvance` (built 2026-09-25: that is the message; the plan first said `E_TOO_LARGE`, which is `assertWholeCore`'s code, not `assertNotWindowed`'s).

- [ ] **Step 2: What "windowed" means here.** When `colIdx` exceeds `maxStorageBufferBindingSize`, P4's residency binds it as a series of arc windows and a kernel reads `colIdx[arc - P.arcBase]`. For a row-walking kernel that is one dispatch per window. For `advance` it is the same, with one extra rule already built into P8-T5's body: a frontier vertex's row may straddle two windows, so each window's dispatch clips every row to `[arcBase, arcEnd)` (`max` / `min` on the row bounds) and emits only the arcs inside it; the per-window dispatches accumulate into the same edge queue through the same `edgeCount` reservation, and `frontierDegreeSum` accumulates across windows rather than being overwritten, or the selector reads a fraction of the real value. The claim kernels are idempotent across windows (`atomicMin`; the bottom-up sweep's `depth[v] == INVALID_INDEX` entry test), so a vertex claimed in window 1 is skipped in window 2.

Built 2026-09-25, a defect the clip alone does not cover: P4's windows do NOT partition the arcs. `planArcWindows` (`src/memory/upload-plan.ts`) opens every window at the previous window's end aligned DOWN to 64 arcs, so two consecutive windows overlap by up to 63 arcs at an unaligned row boundary; a row-range dispatch such as `degree` never revisits the overlap (it belongs to the earlier window's rows), but a frontier-walking kernel sees every frontier entry in every window and clips each row to `[P.arcBase, P.arcEnd)`, so a row inside the overlap would be expanded TWICE. `coreWindows(core)` in `src/primitives/core-shape.ts` is the one place that turns a core into per-window dispatches: window k owns `[start_k, start_{k+1})` (the last one `[start, end)`), which is inside its binding (`start_{k+1} <= end_k`) and partitions `[0, arcCount)` exactly; a window whose owned range is empty dispatches, harmlessly, over nothing. The advance planner, the two fused dispatches, the sweep and `sssp-pred` all take their ranges from it. Watched red: with `arcEnd: w.end` (the overlapping bound) the whole-vertex-set expansion of the faked-limit case differs from the oracle at element 6,013 of the sorted queue.

- [ ] **Step 3: Replace the refusal.** `advance.ts` calls `assertNotWindowed(core, "advance")`; delete the call and record one expand dispatch per window: `windowBinding(core, "colIdx", w)`, one `FRONTIER_PARAMS` record per window that differs from the level's record ONLY in `arcBase` / `arcEnd`, and `dispatchIndirect` from the level's ONE slot (`SLOT.expand` at this level's `slotBase`) for every window. The frontier and its count are the same in every window, so the args are the same; `frontier-finalize` keeps writing exactly seven slots per level and knows nothing about windows (P8-T4 Step 4's `slotBase = level x FRONTIER_CANDIDATES`), and the pinned `FRONTIER_CANDIDATES === 7`, the slot arithmetic and the args buffer size are untouched. The BFS driver does the same for `bfs-fused` (`SLOT.fused` and `SLOT.fusedRetry`, re-issued per window) and `bfs-bottom-up` (`SLOT.bottomUp`). `sssp-pred` is grid-stride, not indirect, so it has no slot at all: its per-window dispatch is still `planGridStride(n)` with `P.stride` from the plan (P8-T6 Step 3), each of its roles once per window, only `arcBase` / `arcEnd` differing. `sssp-relax`, `bf-relax` and `closeness-sweep` KEEP their refusal (`assertWholeCore`, DEP-P8-E): the near-far split and the bit-parallel claim need the whole arc array bound.

The ring, re-counted here as P8-T6 Step 4 asks of every task that adds a record. Each of those per-window dispatches writes its own `FRONTIER_PARAMS` record (only `arcBase` / `arcEnd` differ), so a level is no longer 9 records but `5 + 4 x windows`: the five recorded once per level (`frontier-finalize` twice, `bfs-contract`, the bits `fill`, `bfs-bitset-build`) and the four re-issued per window (`advance-expand`, `bfs-fused`, the fused retry, `bfs-bottom-up`). Against the pinned `RING_SLOTS = 304` the faked-limit run of Step 4 (8 windows, 32 levels per submit) records `32 x (5 + 4 x 8) = 1,184` in one submit, and `UniformRing.reserve` WRAPS to slot 0 rather than throwing (`src/kernel/uniform-ring.ts` line 103): `write()` lands in the host shadow, `flush()` sends the shadow once, and the early levels' dispatches read the late levels' `slotBase`, `arcBase` and `arcEnd` -- wrong depths with no error. So the pin becomes a function of the run: `bfsRingSlots(windows, levelsPerSubmit) = (5 + 4 * windows) * levelsPerSubmit + 16`, exported from `bfs.ts` (`@internal`) beside the comment carrying this count, and the driver calls `algorithmScope(ctx, "breadthFirstSearch", bfsRingSlots(core.windows?.length ?? 1, levelsPerSubmit))` -- `algorithmScope` already takes the slot count per call. At one window and `MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT` it is P8-T6's 304, so nothing else moves. The 16 still covers the per-submit rebuild (10, P8-T8), and the result batch fits at every cadence: it is `19 + windows` records (the iota `fill`, the radix sort's 17, the `pred` `fill`, `sssp-pred` in `MODE 1` once per window), and `(5 + 4 w) x 1 + 16 >= 19 + w` for every `w >= 1`. And the wrap gets a witness, so the next task that adds a record cannot overrun silently again: `UniformRing` gains a readonly `overruns` counter, incremented in `reserve()` when the reservation wraps while a slot is dirty (written since the last `flush()`) -- the one wrap that corrupts, since a wrap after a flush lands behind a `writeBuffer` the queue orders after the submitted batch; `AlgorithmScope` exposes it as `ringOverruns()`; `BfsTuning.onScope` (P8-T6's interface line) hands the scope to the test. Test first, in `test/kernel/uniform-ring.test.ts` beside the existing wrap case: on an 8-slot ring `reserve(5)`, `write` one slot, `reserve(5)` -> `overruns === 1`; then `flush()`, `reserve(5)` (wraps again) -> still 1. Watch it go red without the counter (`undefined` is not 1).

Built 2026-09-25, four facts the text above did not have. (1) The reverse core: `residency.view(s, "reverse")` on an UNDIRECTED windowed core throws `E_TOO_LARGE` (`src/memory/residency.ts` `buildReverse`: "the undirected reverse view is the core, which needs arc windows that no view executes"), so the driver takes the forward core itself as the reverse on an undirected snapshot (graph-format invariant I7, what the view would have returned) and the sweep runs once per FORWARD window; on a directed snapshot the reverse VIEW is never windowed (spec 4.3), so the sweep runs over its one whole binding whatever the forward window count, and a directed snapshot whose reverse adjacency exceeds `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` is refused up front with `E_TOO_LARGE { needed: 4 x arcCount, limit, path: "windowed", algorithm }` -- the residency's own rule for the undirected view, applied before any device work instead of failing at the device's bind-group validation; the bfs test proves it with a caps-proxied context at a 256-byte limit. (2) The overrun rule is EXACT, not "wraps while a slot is dirty": the ring keeps a per-slot dirty mask, and `reserve()` counts an overrun when the reservation covers a slot written since the last `flush()` -- a wrap onto slots an earlier, flushed batch wrote is harmless (its `writeBuffer` is ordered before that batch's submit and the next flush after it) and the coarse rule flagged exactly that in `runAdvance` (three records per batch on a five-slot ring), while a reservation that reaches the dirty slots WITHOUT wrapping is a corruption the coarse rule misses; the ring test holds all four cases. (3) The records as built are fewer than the bound: the contract and the bitset build share one window-free record, a window's two fused dispatches share one, the bits fill has one record per submit, so a level is at most `3 + 3 x windows`; `bfsRingSlots` keeps the plan's `(5 + 4 w) L + 16` as the bound. (4) `advance.record(pass, frontier, level)` no longer takes a window: the planner resolves `coreWindows(core)` once and exposes them as `windows`, and the `arcBase` / `arcEnd` argument checks are gone with the argument.

- [ ] **Step 4: Proof.** The cheap, decisive test is a FAKED limit: `test/primitives/advance.test.ts` binds a core with `maxStorageBufferBindingSize` faked to 1 MiB on a 100k-node graph (`gpuScale()`d), producing at least 8 windows, and asserts the sorted edge queue (the `edgeQueue` buffer) and the three counter words equal the unwindowed run's -- the same technique P4 used for windowed `degree`. Include a hub row longer than one window, because that is the case the clip exists for. `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts` repeats the faked-limit run for the whole traversal through `bfsWithTuning` at the default cadence (`depth`, `parent`, `order` bitwise equal to the unwindowed run) and, holding the scope through `onScope`, asserts `scope.ringOverruns() === 0` after the run -- the assertion that goes red under the pinned 304 (run it once with the pin restored: 1,184 records in 304 slots wrap, and `overruns` counts every wrap in the submit; on that same run the depths are ALSO wrong, which is the silent corruption the counter exists to name). `test/device/constants.test.ts` gains one `it` pinning the ring arithmetic: `bfsRingSlots(1, MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) === 304` (P8-T6's count), `bfsRingSlots(8, MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) === 1200` (this task's faked-limit run), `bfsRingSlots(w + 1, L) - bfsRingSlots(w, L) === 4 * L` (the four per-window kernels named in Step 3: a fifth one must change this pin, so it cannot slip past the count), and `bfsRingSlots(w, 1) >= 19 + w` for `w` in 1..64 (the result batch at cadence 1). Then one real test in `test/limits/advance-windowed.test.ts`: a graph whose `colIdx` genuinely exceeds the device's binding limit, run on the NVIDIA lane (`randomEdges` at the size `test/limits/windowed-200mb.test.ts` uses), `depth` exact against the oracle.

Built 2026-09-25. The faked-limit fixture cannot be "1 MiB on a 100k-node graph" as written: the planner needs `rowPtr` inside the binding too, so the row count is bounded by the limit (`n = limit / 4 - 1`, 262,143 at 1 MiB), and a hub row longer than a window then needs PARALLEL edges (a simple graph's largest degree is `n - 1 < arcsPerWindow`); the fixture is the degree test's `hubbedRandom(n, 10 n, 300,000 x gpuScale() leaves, 20)` (moved to `test/helpers/graphs.ts`): 23 windows on both adapters, the hub row spanning two. `test/primitives/advance.test.ts` expands the hub alone and the whole vertex set through a residency over the faked caps (`withResidency`, `fakeCaps`) and holds the sorted queue and the counters block equal to the unwindowed run's and the oracle's, twice; `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts` traverses from the hub and from a leaf at the default cadence, at cadence 1 and with `alpha: U32_MAX` (which makes Beamer's test switch on the first growing level, so the sweep runs per window too), holds depth / parent / order / the counts bitwise against the unwindowed run and the oracle, and holds `ringOverruns() === 0` on BOTH runs' scopes. Under the pinned 304 the counter read 2,006 on lavapipe (23 windows, one 32-level submit) and depth[2] read `INVALID_INDEX` for 1: the silent corruption named. The ring arithmetic was wrong in a second place the card found: the result batch flushes in its own submit and its size grows with `n`, not the cadence -- the radix sort's four passes record one slot plus the scan's `2 x levels - 1` each, and the scan over the `256 x ceil(n / WG)`-word table has three levels at 262,143 nodes -- so `(5 + 4) x 1 + 16 = 25` slots against the 27 the result batch recorded wrapped over the sort's own records and returned a wrong `order` from the UNWINDOWED run at cadence 1 (the plan's `19 + windows` count had no scan level in it). `bfsRingSlots` is therefore `max((5 + 4 w) L + 16, 34 + w)`, `34 + w` being two fills, four passes of at most eight records (four scan levels cover any `n` below 2^32 at WG 256) and `sssp-pred` per window; the constants test pins 304 at (1, 32), 1,200 at (8, 32), 35 at (1, 1) and (1, 2), the `4 L` increment once `L >= 3` (where the level submit is the larger batch for every `w`), and `>= 34 + w` at every cadence. The node-limits case: 2.5M nodes / 50M arcs in two windows on the RTX 4070 SUPER, 8 levels, 1 switch, 318 ms for the traversal at load 3.

```ts
it("labels a breadth-first search computed on the device as single precision, and its levels are the CPU's", { timeout: SETTLE_MS }, ...)
    // the explicit timeout, as the PageRank case has: a cyclic predecessor chain would hang the seam's unbounded
    // walk, and a hang must read as a failure on the lane, not as a stalled job
    // gpu.session.runs.start("bfs") -> run.status === "succeeded", run.caveats.precision === "f32";
    // the same run on the `acceleration="off"` element (cpu) has precision "f64" and, per node, the same `level`
    // and `order` grouped by level -- PD-14 makes the GPU order deterministic and level-grouped; the CPU's
    // FIFO order differs within a level, so the assertion on `order` is "grouped by level and a permutation of the
    // CPU's per-level set", which is what a reader of the panel sees preserved
it("labels a Dijkstra run computed on the device as single precision, and its route costs the CPU's", { timeout: SETTLE_MS }, ...)
    // gpu.session.runs.start("shortest-path", { method: "dijkstra", source, target }) -> precision "f32"

    // RunsApi.descriptorFor matches canonical keys only, so runs.start("dijkstra") throws E_UNKNOWN_ALGORITHM
    // before any accelerator is consulted -- every existing browser test spells it this way); `distance` per node equal to the cpu element's within
    // 1e-5 relative; `graph.cost` equal within the same tolerance; the GPU's `onPath` nodes and edges form ONE valid
    // path from the source to the target whose edge weights sum to that cost -- NOT node-for-node `onPath` equality
    // with the CPU element: the two backends break ties differently by design (the CPU port's predArc is the relaxing
    // arc under heap order, the GPU's is PD-27's smallest key-step arc), so on a fixture with two equal-cost shortest
    // paths the routes differ while both are correct
```

The file runs only when `GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU` names hardware (its header says why: SwiftShader is slower than the CPU path and never attached), so these cases run on the GPU lane (`gpu.yml` runs the element's browser project there) and on the dev box; on the five CI shards the file skips as it does today. ### Task P8-T14: The `bfs` benchmark group, the append-session script and T-10 on both runner classes

**Depends on P8-T13. Parallel with P8-T15.**

**Spec:** design 10.4 (T-10: BFS on a 1M-node / 10M-edge RMAT of diameter about 10 in 100 ms or less; a 1000 x 1000 grid of roughly 2,000 levels in 1.5 s or less with `mapAsync` calls at most `ceil(levels / 32) + 1`, PD-7); design 10.3's BFS row (2-5 ms at 100k / 1M, 10-30 ms at 1M / 10M, both marked [X] and therefore replaced by a measurement here, design 13 rule (c)); design 11.7 (the baseline files and `bench:compare`); `design/decisions/2026-09-24-performance-targets-belong-to-a-card-class.md`; `scripts/bench-compare.js`'s header (the 1.35x rule, the 2.5 ms floor, the pinned best); G3.md and G4.md appendix A (the append procedure this task turns into a script).

Consumes `bench` (`benchmarks/harness.ts` line 138), `BenchResult` (line 26), `BenchSession` (line 263) and `appendSession` (line 324 -- which writes the runner's OWN `benchmarks/out/<class>.json`, never `benchmarks/results/`; the new script of Step 3 is a different thing with a similar name), `TIERS`, `rmatEdges`, `gridEdges`, `snapshotOf` (`benchmarks/datasets.ts`), `benchmarks/wcc.bench.ts` (the group shape), `LeakCounter`.
