Run: `cd $PKG && pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/benchmarks.test.ts -t append-session`
Expected: FAIL, five cases red. The script is spawned, so node's `Error: Cannot find module '.../scripts/bench-append-session.js'` is the RECEIVED text of each refusal case's `toContain` (`expected 'node:internal/modules/cjs/loader:1386...' to contain 'no session'`), and the accepting case reads `expected 1 to be +0` (a missing module exits 1, the same code as a refusal -- which is why every refusal case asserts the printed reason and not the exit code alone). As built (2026-09-25): the refusals are cases against `test/fixtures/bench/append-session-out.json` plus the real T4 out file already under `test/fixtures/bench/` (seven groups: refused for lacking `attraction-scale`, the first missing name in `REQUIRED_GROUPS` order); the group list is pinned by acceptance of the nine and a loop that removes each one and expects it named.

- [ ] **Step 2: The rows.** BFS on the RMAT ladder (`TIERS` at 100k / 1M and 1M / 10M) top-down (`bfsWithTuning({ direction: "top-down" })`) and direction-optimizing (the public `breadthFirstSearch`), BFS on the 1000 x 1000 grid with the `mapAsync` count measured through `LeakCounter` -- after `await verifyDevice(ctx)` and `counter.resetMapAsync()`, as every counting test in this plan does, or the first printed row reads `k + 2` for the self-check's two maps -- and PRINTED beside the wall time (`[bfs] grid1000 levels=<L> mapAsync=<k>` -- the benchmark row's name carries the level count; the count itself is asserted by `test/limits/bfs-large.test.ts`, never by the benchmark), SSSP on the same two RMAT tiers with random weights in `[0.1, 10]`, and the fused / two-phase / bottom-up level counts from the counters block printed per row. Report wall time around the whole call including upload, as design 10.4 requires (`teardown` releases the snapshot so the next run re-uploads, as `wcc.bench.ts` does). As built (2026-09-25), three facts the repository forced: (a) `rmatEdges` takes a power-of-two scale, so the rungs are `BFS_RMAT_RUNGS` = 2^17 x 8 (`100k/1M`) and 2^20 x 10 (`1M/10M`), undirected, seed 12345, and the BFS snapshots carry NO weight column (the upload the row includes is the adjacency alone; the SSSP snapshots carry the `[0.1, 10)` weights); (b) `LeakCounter` sees only buffers created after it wraps a device and the runner context's staging ring predates it, so the grid row runs on a SECOND device -- `createNodeGpu` + `requestDevice` with the raised limits, wrapped, then adopted by `GpuContext.create({ device })` -- with `verifyDevice` paid before the count, and the row is timed on that context; (c) there is NO profiler row: the traversal drivers record a whole submit (up to 32 levels) as ONE compute pass and never call `profiler.resolveInto`, so no per-kernel GPU time exists to report, and adding it would be a driver change outside this task (the G8 template's "Profiler per pass" line is answered "none: wall only" unless a later task resolves the query set in `bfs.ts`). The per-level choices are printed from an UNTIMED `bfsWithTuning` run with `onLevel` (`[bfs] <row> levels= switches= visited= fused= twoPhase= bottomUp= overflow=`), because `onLevel` adds a readback per submit that the timed row must not carry.

- [ ] **Step 3: The append script, committed.** `scripts/bench-append-session.js` is the G4 appendix A script (`tmp/p4/t14/append-session.mjs`) made real: `node scripts/bench-append-session.js <out file> <results file>` appends the LAST session of `benchmarks/out/<class>.json` to `benchmarks/results/<class>.json`, sorted by date, and REFUSES (exit 1, a sentence naming the reason) a file with no session, a session that ran on a software adapter (`gpu.software`), a session missing any of the nine groups (`REQUIRED_GROUPS`, `bfs` included -- so the pinned baseline stays complete: `bench:compare` reads the LAST session of the out file and compares it with the best of EVERY session in the results file, so a baseline session missing a group would leave that group's rows `new (no baseline)` and unguarded rather than break the comparison), and a session whose date is already in the results file. Plain JavaScript beside `bench-compare.js`, documented in its header with the same "why" style, and the four refusals are cases of `test/benchmarks.test.ts` against fixture files under `test/fixtures/bench/`. The G3 / G4 appendices stay as history; nothing sends a builder to a gitignored scratch directory again.

- [ ] **Step 4: PD-17 -- both runner classes before the commit, and which figure is which.** T-10 is a CONTRACT on the reference card (the RTX 4070 SUPER, class `nvidia-lovelace-driver580`): `<= 100 ms` at 1M / 10M RMAT and `<= 1.5 s` on the grid, and a miss there is not relaxed -- the owner either re-fixes the target in a recorded decision in the pull request or the phase continues with the miss recorded (design 10.4). On `gpu-linux-t4` the same rows are RECORDED figures: the record states them, `README.md`'s T4 table carries them, and a regression on that class is caught by `bench:compare` against the T4's own pinned best, not by the reference card's target. Capture on the card locally with `pnpm run bench` -- EVERY group, not `pnpm run bench bfs`: the append script refuses a session missing any of the nine groups, so a `bfs`-only session can never be appended -- append with the script, then push the branch with the `gpu` label so `gpu.yml` runs `pnpm run bench` on the T4 and uploads `benchmarks/out/gpu-linux-t4.json` as the `gpu-results-<run>` artifact; download it, append with the script, and land BOTH baseline files in the same commit as the benchmark file. A baseline captured on one class and merged alone makes `bench:compare` report every `bfs` row as `new (no baseline)` on the other, which is not a failure but is a gap the record must not have.

- [ ] **Step 5: Replace the [X] numbers.** Design 10.3's BFS column and design 10.1's "BFS scratch" column are extrapolations. Record what was measured, with the load average beside every local timing and the runtime named (`webgpu` 0.4.0, Ubuntu 22.04.5, driver 580.173.02 on the card; the T4's driver from the lane's `gpu-report.json`).

Run: `cd $PKG && eval $GPU_NV pnpm run bench && pnpm run build:all && eval $GPU_NV node scripts/gpu-report.js > gpu-report.json && pnpm run bench:compare && node scripts/bench-append-session.js benchmarks/out/nvidia-lovelace-driver580.json benchmarks/results/nvidia-lovelace-driver580.json && pnpm run bench:compare`
Expected: the tables print (all nine groups; `gpu-report.js` imports `dist/node.js`, hence the build); `bench:compare` reports every `bfs` row `new (no baseline)` on the first run and, once the baseline is appended, `ok` -- a row goes `REGRESSION` only when its median AND minimum both exceed 1.35x the pinned best of every session AND both rose by at least 2.5 ms; the append prints the session's date, class, result count and the nine groups. The T4 leg (the push with the `gpu` label, the artifact download, the second append) is the OWNER's: a subagent never pushes, so the commit of Step 6 carries the reference card's session alone and the T4 session lands in a follow-up commit before the pull request merges (RP-8).

As measured on 2026-09-25 (RTX 4070 SUPER, driver 580.173.02, `webgpu` 0.4.0, load average 1.7-2.1, card idle at 0 % / 2 MiB before the run): `bfs auto at 1M/10M` about 240 ms against the 100 ms contract and the grid row about 5.6 s against 1.5 s -- BOTH MISSED, and the reason is measured, not guessed. The cost is per LEVEL and per CALL, not per submit or per node: the 200 x 200 grid (399 levels) costs 2.85 ms per level at cadences 32, 8 and 1 alike, and a karate BFS (34 nodes, 3 levels) costs 90 ms per call; a CPU profile of twenty karate calls puts 66 % of the time in Dawn-node's `processImmediate` polling (waiting on the device) and 9 % inside `dispatchWorkgroupsIndirect` itself. With Dawn's `skip_validation` toggle (`createNodeGpuContext({ dawnFeatures: ["skip_validation"] })`) the same karate call takes 3.0 ms and the 399-level grid 27 ms (0.07 ms per level): about 97 % of the traversal's wall time on this runtime is Dawn's own validation of `dispatchWorkgroupsIndirect` (an internal validation pass per indirect dispatch), which the frontier design's nine indirect dispatches per level pay nine times a level. The baselines record the DEFAULT runtime, because that is what a consumer gets; the toggle is unsafe and is not used by the benchmark. The decision -- re-fix T-10 to the class, fold the per-level dispatches, or drop to direct dispatch where a count is already known on the host -- is the owner's, for G8 section 7 (`tmp/probe-submit-cost.ts`, `tmp/probe-fixed-cost.ts`, `tmp/probe-karate-loop.ts` under the worktree's gitignored `tmp/` are the probes). The same group on lavapipe (`--allow-software --runs 1`, load 4-9) reads `bfs auto at 100k/1M` 75 ms and `bfs auto at 1M/10M` 449 ms -- a CPU rasteriser within 0.7-1.9x of the card, which is the same fact seen from the other side. On lavapipe the `sssp at 1M/10M` row is ABSENT with a printed reason: the near-far queue binds 8 x roundUp(arcCount, 64) = 168 MB whole against lavapipe's 128 MiB binding limit (`E_TOO_LARGE`, DEP-P8-E), so the group catches that one code and prints `[sssp] sssp at 1M/10M: no row (...)` instead of crashing the run; both runner classes raise the limit to 2 GiB and record the row. The full nine-group run exited 139 (SIGSEGV after `results appended`, the session intact) ONCE in seven runs of the group on the card (the full nine-group baseline run; the six others, three of them after the change below, exited 0); the crash is at process exit, in the class of the P1-T1 Dawn teardown race, and the group now restores the leak counter's proxies before disposing its second device. If `gpu.yml` ever exits 139 at the end of `pnpm run bench`, this is where to look.

- [ ] **Step 6: Commit (owner)** -- `perf(webgpu-graph-algorithms): the BFS and SSSP benchmarks, the append-session script and the T-10 baselines`.

Consumes `withSabotage`, `SABOTAGE`, `SABOTAGE_EXEMPT`, `sabotagedBody` (`test/helpers/sabotage.ts`), `acquireBrowser` / `requireBrowserGpu` (`test/setup/browser.ts`), `writeNoiseFixture` / `recordNoiseRow` (`test/helpers/noise-floor.ts`), `bfsWithTuning`'s `onLevel` (the inspect seam), `test/limits/pagerank-1m.test.ts` (the limits-test shape).

- [ ] **Step 1: Run every mutation, and gate the phase.** Add `"P8"` to `SABOTAGE_PHASES` and update `coverage.test.ts`'s pinned list; from then on every non-exempt P8 kernel (none of the fifteen is exempt) needs at least three rows or the suite is red. The rows were written by the tasks that wrote the kernels, each beside the test it must break; this task runs them all (`test/sabotage/{compact,frontier,advance,bfs,sssp,bellman-ford,closeness}.test.ts`) and closes the gaps `coverage.test.ts` reports. Three per kernel across fifteen kernels is 45 mutations; the tasks wrote 58: `compact-scatter`, `dedupe-claim`, `dedupe-filter` 3 each (9); `frontier-finalize` 6 plus `fused-threshold-inverted` from P8-T7 and `growing-test-inverted` from P8-T8 (8); `advance-expand` 5 plus P8-T12's windowed row (6); `bfs-contract` 3; `sssp-pred` 4 from P8-T6 plus 3 from P8-T9 (7); `bfs-fused`, `bfs-bottom-up`, `bfs-bitset-build`, `bfs-unvisited-flags` 3 each (12); `sssp-relax` 4; `bf-relax` 3; `closeness-sweep` and `closeness-reduce` 3 each (6). A mutation that SURVIVES is a bug in the test suite and blocks the gate exactly as a failing test does -- if one survives, the fix is a better test, never a weaker mutation. Every row's check is a value, a set, a counter word or a dispatch count; the record lists any row whose factor is not `Infinity` with its measured factor. As built: the tasks wrote 58 rows -- exactly the breakdown above, whose `advance-expand` 6 already counts P8-T12's `clip-ignored`; the "59" P8-T12 and P8-T15 first recorded counted that row twice, and P8-T17 re-counted the helper's rows (58) -- no gap was open when `"P8"` joined the list, every row is caught on lavapipe and on the RTX 4070 SUPER, and the one finite factor is `bfs-bottom-up/early-exit-removed` at 253 (the `arcsScanned` ratio of P8-T8, identical on both adapters); the whole `test/sabotage` directory (25 files, 306 tests) runs in 32 s on lavapipe and 156 s on the card.

- [ ] **Step 2: The `inspect()` stage comparisons.** Through `bfsWithTuning({ levelsPerSubmit: 1, onLevel })` and `ssspWithTuning({ roundsPerSubmit: 1, onRound })`, compare level by level against the oracle's own per-level state on `gridEdges(30, 30)`, `rmatEdges(14, 10, 7)` and the weighted karate: the frontier as a SET equals the oracle's vertices at that depth, the counters block's `frontierCount` equals that set's size, `frontierDegreeSum` equals the oracle's degree sum of the previous level, `unvisitedCount` and `unvisitedDegreeSum` after each rebuild equal the oracle's complement (P8-T8 Step 5). This is what catches a wrong expansion that a wrong contraction happens to cancel; end-to-end depth equality would average it away. As built: the BFS comparison is one case of `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts` on `rmatEdges(14, 10, 7)` at cadence 1 under the default (which sweeps bottom-up from level 2) and under `top-down` -- the frontier set, `frontierCount`, `frontierDegreeSum` (the expanded level's out-degree sum, 0 after a bottom-up sweep, `overflowLevels` held at 0 so no retry doubles it) and the two unvisited words against the oracle's complement computed directly, beside P8-T6's grid30 set case and P8-T8's rmat16 model case. The SSSP comparison cannot be "the oracle's per-level state": Dijkstra has no rounds and the near-far block is schedule-dependent (P8-T9 Step 6), so the weighted-karate case (at `delta: 1`, so the ladder has ten steps: 23 rounds of which 10 far on lavapipe, farthest distance 10.09) holds what IS a function of the settled distances -- the level word counts rounds (`k + 1` after submit `k`, the done boundary dispatches nothing), `done` rises only at the last, `thresholdBits` climbs the f32 delta ladder by exactly one add per far round and never otherwise, and the far-round count equals the ladder step above the oracle's farthest finite distance (every node below the threshold settles from the near pile; the near-round count is only bounded by `reachedCount`). The `stages` array under `GRAPHTY_GPU_INSPECT=1` was NOT built: `onLevel` / `onRound` at cadence 1 already hand every per-level readback to the caller, `ctx.debug.inspect` is read by no traversal driver, and a `stages` member would widen the public result types for a debugging convenience; a human prints the `onLevel` arguments.

- [ ] **Step 3: The twins at three subgroup sizes.** Three P8 kernels have a subgroup variant (`needs: ["subgroups"]`): `advance-expand` (it calls `wg_scan_u32`), `bfs-bottom-up` and `bfs-unvisited-flags` (they call `wg_reduce_u32`, P8-T8 Step 2 and Step 4). The two BFS kernels' helper call is a sum, so the BFS suite must read bitwise-equal `depth`, `arcsScanned` and unvisited words on and off; `advance-expand`'s scan is the one whose LANE order the twin proves. Run its suite against both variants in the same process (a second context from `acquire({ subgroups: false })`) on lavapipe (size 8), SwiftShader (size 4, the browser project's `compile-matrix.test.ts` case) and NVIDIA (size 32), asserting bitwise-identical SORTED queues -- these are `u32`, so "within a tolerance" does not arise. The CI twin pass (`GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS=1` over `test/primitives test/layouts test/algorithms`) covers the new suites without a workflow edit. As built: `test/primitives/advance.test.ts` already ran every fixture on both contexts (P8-T5); the BFS side is a new case of `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts` that traverses `rmatEdges(16, 10, 3)` at cadence 1 on the feature context and on `acquire({ subgroups: false })` and holds `depth`, `parent`, `order`, `switches` and EVERY counters-block word of every submit equal (`arcsScanned` and the unvisited words are the sums the two twin kernels fold), on both adapters. SwiftShader's size-4 leg stays the compile-matrix case: the browser project runs no advance suite.

- [ ] **Step 4: The browser smoke.** Add to `test/browser/algorithms.test.ts` the BFS case design 11.6 item 4 names: `breadthFirstSearch` on karate from vertex 0 in Chromium, `depth` identical to `bfsOracle`'s, `order` grouped by level, `switches === 0`; and one `sssp` case on the weighted karate, `dist` bitwise equal to the f32 oracle. Update the file's header (the "deliberately absent" sentence).

- [ ] **Step 5: The `node-limits` tests.** `test/limits/bfs-large.test.ts`: the 1000 x 1000 grid unscaled from the corner (1,999 levels; `depth` against the oracle; `mapAsyncCalls === Math.ceil(levels / MAX_LEVELS_PER_SUBMIT) + 1` after `verifyDevice` and a reset, the gate's T-10 accounting) and the 1M / 10M RMAT BFS (a real 80 MB edge queue; `depth` against the oracle; `switches > 0`). `test/limits/sssp-1m.test.ts`: the 1M / 10M RMAT with random weights, `dist` bitwise against the f32 oracle, `expectTriangleInequality`. Mark both in `test/limits/README.md`. As built on the RTX 4070 SUPER (load 3-4): the grid traverses in 6.4 s with `mapAsync` exactly 64 (`ceil(1999 / 32) + 1`) after `verifyDevice` on a `LeakCounter`-wrapped raw device (the shape of the bfs suite's gate case), depth exact, `switches` 0; the 1M / 10M rung (`rmatEdges(20, 10, 12345)` from `benchmarks/datasets`, the benchmark's own rung and seed, 84 MB of edge queue at the default capacity) reaches 577,681 nodes in 6 levels with 2 switches in 1.8 s from a fresh context (upload and first-call compile included; the benchmark's timed row is 237 ms); `sssp-1m` reweights the same rung with the benchmark's `makeRandom(12345)` in [0.1, 10) and is bitwise the f32 oracle in 0.55 s.

- [ ] **Step 6: The noise-floor rows.** Append to `benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json` through `recordNoiseRow`: the f32-versus-f64 Dijkstra spread (`sssp-relax.f32-vs-f64`, the basis of P8-T9's derived tolerance -- ALREADY recorded by P8-T9 together with its tolerance, because that task's differential reads them; re-record it only if the fixture list changes); the cross-adapter spread of `dist` and of `depth` (expected to be exactly zero -- record the zero, because a later non-zero is then a finding rather than a surprise); the `advance-expand` twin spread (zero). `test/noise-floor.test.ts` checks the committed rows on every run. The runtime line of every row: `webgpu` 0.4.0 on Ubuntu 22.04.5. As built: three bitwise members (`P8_NOISE_SET`) whose fixtures the suites write under `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1` -- `bfs-contract / rmat14-depth` (row `bfs.depth.cross`, 16,329 values), `sssp-relax / grid30-integer-dist` (row `sssp-relax.dist.cross`, 900 f32 bit patterns) and `advance-expand / karate-reversed-queue` (rows `advance-expand.cross` and `advance-expand.twin`, 156 sorted queue entries, the twin fixture written by the same case) -- and the rows were recorded by the noise-floor test's own write run on lavapipe from the fixtures of both adapter classes: every row 0 / 0. No tolerance is derived from them (bitwise members carry `NO_ROWS` tolerances); the file's `recordedAt` moved and the `sssp-relax.f32-vs-f64` row changed position only. The write run also rewrites this adapter's two FA2 karate fixtures in pretty-printed form with identical values; they were restored from HEAD.

Run: `cd $PKG && eval $GPU_LLVM pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/sabotage && eval $GPU_NV pnpm exec vitest run --project=node-limits && GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=swiftshader GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any node scripts/run-browser-project.js`
Expected: every mutation caught; limits green; browser green (exit 124 with `numFailedTests === 0` counts as green, design 11.6). Write the load average beside the limits timings. The three new suite cases and the fixture writers run under `-t "P8-T15"` of `test/algorithms/bfs.test.ts test/algorithms/sssp.test.ts test/primitives/advance.test.ts` on both adapters, and the noise-floor rows are recorded by `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1 ... --no-file-parallelism test/noise-floor.test.ts` after both classes' fixtures exist.

### 7.3 Risk register for this plan

| Id   | Risk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Mitigation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| RP-1 | The seam changes under the phase (a port of `bellmanFord` or `closenessCentrality` lands in `@graphty/algorithms` with a different option type).                                                                                                 | P8-T13's conformance lines fail the strict-consumer compile the moment the types diverge, and the fix is in the GPU member, never a mirror.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| RP-2 | The block-mapped expansion does not compile because a barrier sits inside a guard, or the strip loop's bound is judged non-uniform. This is the most common WGSL failure in this package and it costs hours when it is diagnosed as a logic bug. | P8-T5 Step 2 names it before the code, with the `select`-based guard and the `workgroupUniformLoad` bound written out.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| RP-3 | `dist` turns out NOT to be bitwise reproducible (an adapter whose f32 add is not correctly rounded), which would invalidate PD-9 and PD-10 and the exactness of the SSSP test.                                                                   | The argument is written out in P8-T9 Step 2 and it rests on two claims -- every candidate is eventually offered, which the near-far loop guarantees, and the add is correctly rounded, which is measured on both adapters. If a measurement contradicts it, the f32 comparison drops to the derived tolerance and the finding goes in G8; nothing else in the phase changes. |
| RP-4 | Design 9.7's `1e-5` for `dist` is tighter than f32 accumulation allows on a high-diameter fixture.                                                                                                                                               | P8-T2 Step 4 measures the spread before any kernel exists, so the number is known at the start of the phase rather than discovered by a red test at the end. Re-fixing is an owner decision in the pull request, never a quiet edit to a test file.                                                                                                                          |
| RP-5 | Nine tasks append to `src/kernels.ts`, so nothing in the middle of the phase can run in parallel.                                                                                                                                                | PD-2 states the rule; the parallel opportunities are at the two ends (P8-T1 / T2 / T3 at the start, P8-T14 / T15 at the finish) and P8-T12 beside P8-T9..T11.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| RP-6 | A mutation whose answer stays right (the bottom-up early exit, the fused threshold, the growing test) survives because nothing measures it.                                                                                                      | Each is pinned to a counter word the tests read from the block (`arcsScanned`, `fusedLevels`, the `direction` sequence) -- never a timing (change-log item (i)).                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| RP-7 | Eighty new test cases push the default lane past T-12's 15 minutes on lavapipe.                                                                                                                                                                  | P8-T17 Step 1 records the wall time; the large fixtures are sized by `gpuScale()` and the genuinely large ones live in `node-limits`, which the default lane does not run.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| RP-8 | The 1.35x / 2.5 ms `bench:compare` gate reads the `bfs` rows as `new` on the class whose baseline was not appended, and a later regression there goes unseen.                                                                                    | PD-17: both classes' sessions land in one commit (P8-T14 Step 4), through the committed append script.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| RP-9 | The device self-check's two extra `mapAsync` calls make the gate's readback count red on a fresh device.                                                                                                                                         | Every counting test awaits `verifyDevice` and resets the counter first (Global Constraints; P8-T6 Step 5).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

### 8.2 Placeholder scan

No task says "TBD", "as appropriate" or "etc.". Every Run line names a real script from `webgpu-graph-algorithms/package.json` (`build:all`, `lint`, `test:node`, `test:limits`, `coverage`, `bench`, `bench:compare`, `scripts/run-browser-project.js`) or a real vitest project (`node`, `node-limits`, `browser`). Every helper named in a task exists today on master under that name (section 0.1 lists the greps), except the ones a task creates and names as produced. The `{{OPEN: ...}}` markers live only in appendix 7.4, which is the template P8-T17 fills.

### 8.3 Type consistency across the tasks

`GpuBfsResult`, `GpuSsspResult` and `GpuBellmanFordResult` are declared once, in P8-T1, spelled from design 3.3 lines 830-832. `BfsOptions`, `SsspOptions` and `HitsOptionsLike` are never declared in this package: they are `import type`d from `@graphty/algorithms` and re-exported by `src/types/accelerator.ts`, and `test/types/conformance.test-d.ts` holds the members' parameter types equal to the seam's. P8-T6, P8-T9, P8-T10, P8-T11 and P8-T13 consume them and none redeclares. `GpuScoresResult` for closeness comes from `src/types/algorithms.ts` and is not redeclared. `Frontier`, `CompactPlanner` and `AdvancePlanner` are each declared in exactly one file under `src/primitives/` and imported everywhere else. The counters block and the params block are defined once, in `src/kernels.ts` by P8-T4 (grown by nothing later: every field a later task reads is declared there), and every kernel that reads a counter reads that block by index through the `W` record of `src/primitives/frontier.ts`.

### 8.4 The arithmetic, checked

Fifteen kernels (section 0.4 PD-4 and the table of P8-T4 Step 2: three primitives' bodies, one selector, one expansion, three BFS claims, two BFS helpers, one predecessor pass, one relax, one Bellman-Ford relax, two closeness). Nine appends to `src/kernels.ts` (P8-T3 .. P8-T11). Seven indirect slots per level (`FRONTIER_CANDIDATES`). Twenty-four words in the counters block, 96 bytes; twenty fields in the params block, 80 bytes. Fifty-eight sabotage rows (P8-T15 Step 1 lists them kernel by kernel) against a floor of forty-five. Six constants and one WGSL helper pair. Five decision records. Eighteen commits. Twelve gate items. 22.5 engineer-days.
